# Open questions, risks, and unverified claims

This file lists every open question, risk, and unverified claim in the design that an implementer must not assume. Each row gives the question in one sentence, where the design states it, what depends on it, and the work package in `build/BUILD-PLAN.md` that settles it. "No work package" means no gate row or planned step answers it, and the row says who can.

Evidence tags carry over from the source: [inference], [guess], [unverified], and [unknown], plus [measured] and [doc] where Milestone 0 has answered part of a row. Rows marked "found by the build plan" are sequencing questions that `build/BUILD-PLAN.md` raised while placing work. They are [inference] and not in the design. Rows marked "from a walk report" are questions that a writer of the implementability walk left open in `history/review/walk-fix-reports.md`. Rows marked "from a third-walk report" are questions that writers X1, X2, and Y of the third walk left open in `history/review/walk-rest-reports.md`.

Sources: "Open questions and risks" in `design/DESIGN.md` and `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, every `[unverified]` tag in `design/`, the pending Cloudflare-design decisions (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Four Cloudflare-design questions still carry the run's defaults", which copies `history/FRAME.md`, "Pending operator decisions for the Cloudflare design"), the flip conditions in `alternatives/AWS.md`, "When the AWS design would be the better choice", the flip conditions in `design/DESIGN.md` and `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Synthesis decision", and the writer reports of the implementability walk (`history/review/walk-fix-reports.md`) and of the third walk (`history/review/walk-rest-reports.md`). The walk answered the build plan's earlier sequencing questions R4 to R8, and `design/DESIGN.md`, "Milestones 1 to 7", now places that work (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Implementability walk").

## Foundation and protocol

| Id | Question | Stated in | What depends on it | Settled by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | Is the Raft implementation the team must write (leases, transfer, `suspect`, pre-vote, joint consensus, learners, and `verify`) linearizable on Durable Objects? Partly measured: the M0-WP2 prototype (no joint consensus, no `verify` of real commands) passed Porcupine on 270,040 operations in 2 hours on Cloudflare under resets, transfers, and dropped and failed calls, and on six local runs. A 4-hour Cloudflare run then failed [measured]: a leader that reset and resumed its term reissued ticket numbers, so an `outcome` returned a newer command's result before the old command committed. `Ticket` now carries the issuing instance's `boot` (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, "B0-11: the 4-hour run found a linearizability violation"). The 48-hour run on the fixed code then passed [measured]: 0 violations on 5,742,736 operations under 5,729 member resets and 2,342 transfers, and 0 of 1,814,873 acknowledged writes lost, with each interval widened 100 ms for clock offset between the client colos; on the recorded clocks 72 cross-colo reads fail by 27 to 94 ms, all explained by one offset per colo, none within one colo, and none an ack before commit (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, "B0-11: the 48-hour run"). M0's note that dropping no-reply writes "can only make the check stricter" is wrong for Illegal: a dropped write that a read saw makes a linearizable history look Illegal. B0-11's verdict stands on the fixed checker, because none of its 4 no-reply writes was observed (`tests/m1/results/porcupine-seg.md`). Still open: joint consensus and `verify` of real commands. M1-WP8's 24-hour rerun, `lin24`, lost no acknowledged write and was Ok on every key once its clocks were widened or its lost client rows restored, but did not pass, because it could not rule out an early ack. The second rerun, `lin24b`, passed [measured]: 0 Porcupine violations on 2,850,523 operations (widened 100 ms and 153 ms, and on the recorded clocks once lost client rows were restored or 1 to 4 ms ties allowed), 0 of 904,294 acknowledged writes lost, and 0 violations of the member-side ack check (`tests/m1/results/m1-wp8.md`, "The 24-hour half, 2026-11-04"; `tests/m1/lin/raw/lin24b.report.json`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "F44 answered: the fast acks were a client-clock artifact") | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" and "Tradeoffs accepted"; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Four Cloudflare-design questions", question 3 (default: yes, gated by the 48-hour run, with candidate 1 as the fallback) | Every region-loss and continent-loss guarantee, and RPO zero for every `Tri` chain | M0-WP2 (B0-11), rerun in M1-WP8 (BM-1, 24 hours) |
| F2 | Does flip condition 1 fire? Its latency clause is answered [measured]: 23 one-minute B0-01 runs gave `Tri` commit p99 399 to 690 ms, set by the network, and neither pipelining nor a WebSocket transport cleared 400 ms (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`). The operator kept the base on 2026-10-28, with B0-01 passing at 600 ms and the clause at 1 s (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Keep `Tri` and gate commit p99 at 600 ms"). The linearizability clause is answered too [measured]: a 4-hour run found one violation, a design bug in `Ticket` now fixed (F1), and the 48-hour run on the fixed code passed with a 100 ms clock allowance across client colos (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, "B0-11: the 48-hour run"). Flip condition 1 does not fire. Fault coverage on site C is thin (F36) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Synthesis decision", flip condition 1; `alternatives/AWS.md`, condition 1 | The choice of base. If it fires, candidate 1 becomes the base after its standby-loss stall is fixed, and an operator who needs RPO zero across regions after that has it in AWS Paired [inference] | M0-WP2 (B0-01, B0-11) |
| F3 | Do clocks inside Workers and Durable Objects drift by less than 500 ms, as lease safety assumes [guess]? Reopened by `lease-clock`. Between idle members Milestone 0 measured offsets within 52 ms (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, L0-12). A busy isolate's own `Date.now()` is not drift but lag: it is the time of the isolate's last I/O poll, and a probe read it 715 to 1,681 ms behind a peer's stamp under 400 ms CPU turns, still 654 to 1,588 ms after an awaited storage call. L0-12 m3fr5b read a busy leader 1.17 s off Cloudflare's clock [measured, `tests/am1/results/lease-clock.md`; `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Rows m3fr5b"]. `lease-clock` floors every member clock by peer and edge stamps, which cut the probe's error to 7 to 139 ms, and reports that clock on `/status`. Finding (L0-12 m3fr6, `graft-m3f`, 2026-11-15, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Rows m3fr6"): on the floored clock under the A1 replay with leader kills, the members were 56 to 512 ms apart (`lowerMs` / `upperMs`, the spread between members in one sampling round, each sample widened by half its round trip, rounds with a round trip over 200 ms rejected), and 324 and 228 ms apart at rest before and after the stall; against Cloudflare's clock they were up to 972 ms off on the 76 kept samples (6,289 ms raw over all 97, driver offset 5,335 ms) [measured]. The operator's decision of 2026-11-16 judges L0-12's clock line on the spread between members, the quantity `DRIFT` bounds, so the row is met at 90%; the offset from Cloudflare's clock is not a lease input and stays a finding here. Open: whether the 972 ms is the members' clocks or the probe's own error under load; the remaining all-saturated case is F75 | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; Shape, `DRIFT` | `LEASE` and `DRIFT` for leader leases, `LogEntry.at`, and the overlap bound on Exclusive path leases | M0-WP12 (L0-12's offset line on the `lease-clock` site day) |

| F26 | From a third-walk report: does the runner's broker listener back off and keep its session when the broker answers 503 with `Retry-After: 15`? **Answered [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, BF-54]:** yes. Runner 2.338.2 retries a 503 after the `Retry-After`, 15 s, for up to 4 attempts, then backs off a random 0 to 15 s (11,893 back-offs, max 14.998 s) and starts again. In the whole run, 2,495 of 2,500 runners got 503s with 0 `RunnerSessionInvalid`, 0 recreated sessions, and 0 restarts. So DESIGN's "15 to 30 s" was wrong, and DESIGN now says what was measured | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Identity", `BROKER_HELD_MAX`) | Runners past `BROKER_HELD_MAX` on one broker scope, and the job start time for them | Settled by BF-54. The job start time for them is F76 |

After `logrelay:1114120` was aborted on graft-m4a (2026-11-14), the runner noticed on its next send about 27 s later and reconnected within 1 s to a fresh relay with no tail; on the printed rerun (job 1114123) it reconnected about 29 to 33 s later, and both jobs' `/logs` held all 600 counter lines. | F34 | Does a drain move every group led on a gray site within 20 s (B0-07)? Not yet [measured]: with 50% of calls to and from site C hanging for 3 s, `SiteHealth` on both other sites flagged C within 13.7 s and the drain-key drain committed, but groups led on C moved at p50 14.6 to 20.8 s and max 55 to 68 s after the drain, over three runs with 0 failed writes (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, B0-07). Root cause confirmed and fixed in the prototype: a leader learned of its drain only on a request, half of which hung, and its transfers were abandoned. With an alarm-driven drain check, transfers that retry within their lease, and a wait for the other follower's transfer ack, groups led on C that served a request in the 50 s before the drain moved at p50 5.4 to 6.3 s, 229 of 230 within 20 s, over three runs. The gate now counts groups active in the 50 s before the drain from the drain, and other groups from their next request, under the operator's delegation of 2026-10-30 (`FRAME.md`); 50 s sits inside the leader's 60 s alarm life. Open: one active group at 20.2 s, not explained. On the Rust host (M1-WP3's transfer, the drain checked on every tick) with test-faults stand-ins for `graft-ops`'s drain and `SiteHealth`, 235 of 235 groups moved within 20 s over 3 days, max 17.2 s [measured] (`tests/m0/results/carried.md`, B0-07). Still open: the same with `graft-ops`'s drain key and `DRAIN_BURN` | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Commit and read paths" (journey 5, Automatic drain), Shape (`transfer`, `SiteMode`), Milestone 0 | The 20 s move target for a gray region | M0-WP4 (B0-07) |
| F35 | Why do leaders on a healthy site see 3 or more storage confirmations of 509 to 1,018 ms within 10 s while a peer site's members reset, enough to trip `STEP_DOWN`? Answered [measured]: Durable Object storage stalls on its own. A bare object with no Raft code, writing and syncing at a member's rate, stalled about every 5 s, and 7 of 168 such objects hit 3 or 4 stalls in one window; the rate varies tenfold by hour and does not follow the fault. The count is now 6, and a `STEP_DOWN` target must be outside `RESET_QUARANTINE`; B0-06 then passed 9 of 9. Still open: the stall's mechanism, and whether the 5% commit-error threshold can tell stall-driven loss from a sick link | `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "STEP_DOWN's 3-slow-confirmations rule and B0-06"; `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, `Health` | B0-06's storage half, and `STEP_DOWN.confirm_p99_ms` | M0-WP3 (B0-06) |
| F36 | Did every isolate of the 48-hour B0-11 run inject its member-call faults? A fault-plan reader whose first fetch never settled injects nothing, and the run's code records no per-isolate counters. Lost-transfer rates of 9.4% on A and 9.0% on B fit injection; C has 4 of 64 and its longest gap, 23.7 h, has about a 17% chance at a true 5% rate [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "Audit: fault-plan reader"] | `tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, B0-11 | How much fault load the linearizability verdict covers on site C | The final `/stats` after the run carried no `InjectedFault` lines, so it cannot settle this. C's full-run lost-transfer rate was 9.0% (10 of 111) against A 10.4% and B 7.5%, with a longest no-loss stretch of 21.4 h over 45 transfers (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp2.md`, "B0-11: the 48-hour run"). Settled only by B0-11's rerun in M1-WP2 with per-isolate counters. That rerun, `lin24`, recorded counters in all 88 isolates, each with at least 121 injected faults, on all three sites [measured, `tests/m1/lin/raw/lin24.isolates.json`]; the 48-hour run's own coverage on C stays unknown |
| F37 | Should the commit-error rule need a minimum count of failed calls per follower, as the confirm rule does? Milestone 0 answered the follow-on step-down after a kill [measured]: a new leader now counts a follower's failures only after that follower has answered it in its term, and 0 of 90 groups changed leader again from the killed member (was 7 of 60). Left open: 3 of 90 tripped on 1 to 3 deadline misses in 10 to 30 calls to a healthy far follower, and one change at 16.5 s is unexplained. Also open, folded in from L0-12: should a new leader hold the commit-error rule for one `STEP_DOWN` window (10 s) after winning, not only until `ready`? In L0-12 `t4` `flagged`, a far-region member (c, SIN) won after kill 1, then a won term 5 at +160 s and stepped down 7 s later on commit errors of 0.17 and 0.30, with the heard-from rule applied and at least 10 calls per follower; 2 follow-on changes in 10 kills [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, fourth pass, "Fourth-pass proposed design edits" 14]. LF-12 `r14`'s placed phase then stepped down at exactly 0.05/0.06 and moved the leader to SIN, and at 0.10/0.70 twelve seconds into a phase (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, sixth pass, edit 19). **Answered yes, 3 [measured, M0-WP4]:** `STEP_DOWN.commit_fail_min` is 3. With it, leader kills under load gave 0 follow-on changes in 90 groups against 2 in 90 without it, and leaders left a site failing 30% of calls in 2.9 to 4.1 s and slow storage in 2.8 to 4.2 s (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "`STEP_DOWN` target on the leader's calls and the other follower's probe; F37 as `min3-`"). Left open: failing writes were not run with the count, and a quiet group needs about 10 calls per follower a window at 30% errors to collect 3 [inference]; the hold-for-one-window question is moot while the count suppresses those trips. M1-WP3's simulator, at about 4 calls per follower a second, moved leaders off a site failing 30% of calls in p50 5.1 s, max 12.0 s, against Milestone 0's 2.9 to 4.1 s, because the third failure comes at about 2.5 s [measured, simulator, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`]; the confirm rule's matching limit is F41 | `tests/m0/results/m0-wp4.md`, "Leader kill" and "F37 as `min3-`"; `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, `Health` | `STEP_DOWN.commit_errors` and `commit_fail_min`, and L0-12's feed p99 after kills | M0-WP4 (answered); failing writes and quiet groups by M1-WP3 |
| F38 | Should a reset leader's own pre-vote be refused for RESET_QUARANTINE after its boot while another member leads? A killed leader resets, and 50 to 90 s later it wins leadership back by election once a follower's promise to the new leader lapses (L0-12 `t3`: 3 of 10 kills, `vendor` twice and `human` once, with 0 `STEP_DOWN` in all 10). RESET_QUARANTINE needs two boots or a `stepped_down_at`, so one reset does not hold it off [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, third pass, L0-12 and "Third-pass proposed design edits" 12] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Leadership", `RESET_QUARANTINE`) | A second leader change, and the feed and commit tails around it, 50 to 90 s after every leader kill | M0-WP4, by a test of that rule |
| F39 | Can `outcome` calls be folded into the `commit` call, held until the entry commits, so the leader serves fewer requests without moving where `Acked` is built? About 270 to 330 of a B0-33 leader's 750 to 950 requests/s are `outcome` calls, and in 2 of 3 fifth-pass runs the leader object fell behind in its first 20 s and was lost by election [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, fifth pass, B0-33 and "Proposed design edits (fifth pass)" 36]. Answered yes by M0-WP9's sixth pass [measured]: the leader holds `commit` up to `COMMIT_HOLD` (1 s, [guess]) and answers the outcome, which is still built by `outcome`'s logic once the quorum rule holds. Leader requests fell to 459 to 498/s and `outcome` calls to 1 to 10/s, with up to 576 calls held at once, and 2 of 3 far-flung runs held their leader (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, sixth pass). Left open: `COMMIT_HOLD`'s value, under which 0.1 to 0.2% of commands fell back in a steady run and 9% across a transfer | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape (the write path, `outcome`) and Scale ("Agent fleet, group commands") | B0-33's zero-leader-change line, `mitigated` until the leader's request rate falls | M0-WP9, sixth pass (answered); `COMMIT_HOLD` by M1-WP2 on real code |
| F40 | What slows a leader's AppendEntries to both followers mid-phase with no fault injected, and why does a fresh group's far-site member time out its first 10 to 17 s of AppendEntries under 5,000 arriving pollers? In LF-12 `r18`'s placed phase, from 160 s and again from 240 s, the leader's AppendEntries to both followers failed 51 times and 71 took over 500 ms, all 10 learners lagged together, 25,350 polls took the last resort, and commit p99 reached 1,790 ms with 0 step-downs and 0 polls on voters. Opening bursts of deadlines to the SIN member came in `r16`, `r17`, and `r18`, with and without learners, so feeding newly placed learners is ruled out [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, seventh pass, "Seventh-pass proposed design edits" 21]; the cause is not identified [inference: 5,000 pollers arriving within 10 s while the far member's object is created]. The member counters do not show it, and `learnerCalls` hit its 3,000-record cap at 50 s. **Answered [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, eighth to tenth passes]:** the slow windows are Durable Object storage confirm stalls, 0.5 to 2.1 s on the follower the quorum waits for and up to 3.6 s on the leader, whose output gate holds its sends behind the stalled write [inference for the gate], plus 100 to 250 ms of outbound queueing at the leader and the far follower's round trip. r18 matches that signature by analogy. Two design rules turned a stall into a failure, and both are now fixed: a commit-error `STEP_DOWN` on deadlines caused by the leader's own confirm (`r28`, `farhold-r30`), now excluded in `Health`, and a 2 s client retry after an all-lagging 429 (`r27`), now `Retry-After` of `FEED_BATCH_ALL` in the feed block. Event-loop lag cannot be measured under the Workers clock, and the far member's opening deadline bursts did not recur once members were created before load; their cause stays unknown | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Build order" (the LF-12 row) and Shape (feed block); `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, `Health` | LF-12's placed-phase commit line, and whether held polls or learner feeding can stall a leader | M0-WP12, eighth to tenth passes (answered) |

Twice the clients (26.8 acked writes/s) gave p50 10.8 s and max 21.1 s, so the confirm rate sets it, not the write rate [measured, simulator, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp3.md`, "Liveness against Milestone 0" and design problem 1]. | F65 | What bounds a member's memory under concurrent requests? A Durable Object isolate has 128 MB, and nothing caps the reads, held feed polls, and `suspect` reports a member serves at once. At 500 agents, after the agent push's read was narrowed (`ccd10b1`) and whole-tier reads were packed and trimmed to about 150 KB (`eb8eb0b`, `a271fb3`), repository members still peaked at 263 to 318 MiB, with thousands of `exceededMemory` outcomes, `/suspect` storms of 63,000 to 129,000 on the leader, leader churn, and once a group left with no leader for about 10 minutes until a manual `/campaign` [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rerun2.md`, sections 3, 4, and 14]. Cause [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`]: whole-tier reads in flight on the leader (wasm memory 10.5 MB at 1, 59 MB at 40, 92 MB at 60 on a 1.18 MB dump, reset at 100; held polls changed nothing), turned into `suspect` reports when they missed. Fixed on `lm1-memory` by a per-tier-state dump cache, `HEAVY_READS` with an `overloaded` answer the lookup never reports, and damped reports (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "F65: a leader caches its packed dump"). Narrowed, not answered, 2026-11-11: the 500-agent smoke held every member under 80 MB, but B0-29 took the apac leader to 262 MB and about 600 cheap reads completed in one millisecond after a stall, then `exceededMemory`. Next hypothesis [unverified]: one quorum round grants every waiting read and the host builds all their answers before sending any (600 parallel `{"q":{}}` reads took an idle leader from 55.8 to 94.1 MB). The group left with no leader was its leader stuck 641 s building dumps for about 730 abandoned reads, not an election fault [measured, same]. Open until the 500-agent rows pass hosted. A 740 KB tier answer built as a `serde_json::Value` peaked at 24 MB per read [measured, local allocator harness] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Scale (journey 9, item 1); `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, Scale (item 4) | LM-1's 500-agent rows (B0-29, B0-31 to B0-35, L0-12's A1 replay), and any hot group's availability under load | Branch `lm1-memory` on `graft-m2b`: member memory against reads in flight at 10, 30, and 60 in parallel, then a cap on in-flight reads per member, one member per tier read, and collapsed `suspect` reports, as the measurement points; rounds 2 to 4 capped every read in flight, moved API reads to followers and learners, and stopped shed reads from reaching the leader, which now holds (`build/BUILD-PLAN.md`, AM1-WP6); round 5 moved the owner's `Authz` fold and `o/r` reads to its learners and followers, which cut the 500-agent smoke's commit p99 from 50,066 to 1,739 ms with no member over 128 MB, and no row passes yet; round 6 on the remaining limits (`build/BUILD-PLAN.md`, AM1-WP6, "What LM-1 still needs"; F67), then LM-1's 500-agent rows; learner-dumps (2026-11-12) let learners serve whole-tier reads past `HEAVY_READS` from one shared dump: 94.7% of `read:api` answered with 8 learners, at edge p99 5.0 s with the member read p99 1.2 to 1.3 s, so the edge's 68 ms of CPU and about 2 s of wall per 1 MB answer now set the API latency [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`]; narrow-reads (2026-11-12) answers every REST list and object `GET` and every GraphQL query but login-filtered ones and mutations from the rows its reply renders, byte-identical to the whole-tier reply (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`narrow-reads`: REST and GraphQL reads answer only the rows their reply renders"). On `graft-m3c` this served 99.8% of `read:api`, against 93.1%, and cut the edge's CPU per answer from 66 ms to 11 to 13 ms at p50, but API p99 only fell from 5.0 to 4.4 s [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads.md`]. The next limit is the token, name, and `Authz` reads before the tier read, about 1.2 to 1.4 s in sequence on an idle edge, queued at 200 reads/s [inference]; narrow-reads-2 (2026-11-12) reads them at once and caches them per isolate for `READ_CACHE`, against a new journey 9 API target of p99 at most 2 s on B0-29: API p99 fell from 4.9 to 3.15 s (p50 1.5 to 0.66 s) at 99.9% served, the identity stages are 0 ms at p50, and the learners' tier read now sets the p99 at 2.2 to 2.6 s on the edge [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-2.md`]; the gate is not met. narrow-reads-3 (2026-11-13) found the wait was each waited read redoing its read-index; a read is now answered at or above the commit index the leader confirmed for it with no second round, which took the tier read's edge p99 from 2.9 to 1.7 to 2.0 s, and API p99 to 2.1 s (99.97%) and 3.3 s (99.9%) on two clean runs on `graft-m3e`, the second set by cold edge isolates' token, name, `Authz`, and `Release` genesis reads (1.2 to 3.8 s) [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`]; the gate is not met on every run. Two 500-agent runs on the same build with a leader change had voters at 280 to 286 MiB and `exceededMemory`, learners at most 93 MiB. The genesis now rides the edge's binding. On the final tree (main 84d29c2 merged), six clean 500-agent runs missed the gate: ENTRY_SOFT put commit p99 at 2.9 s against 949 ms with `ENTRY_MAX`, and far learners' `late` answers (an apply wait of up to 2.1 s after the leader's `ReadIndexRes`) held API p99 at 2.5 to 3.1 s. A learner hedge on the edge served fewer reads and was reverted (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Not adopted: the edge hedging a slow learner read"), and so was pipelining appends to learners, which left the far learners' wait where it was and took the leader to 126 MiB ("Not adopted: pipelining AppendEntries to learners"). A per-read split (gate6, API p99 3.2 s at 98.4% served) showed the far learners' wait is for the entries their read index names, which reached them a call later in 82 to 87% of batches (p99 1.9 to 4.5 s), so the leader now sends those entries ahead of its `ReadIndexRes` ("A read-index answer follows the entries the asker needs"). Next: that change's gate runs, then the near learners' own apply behind storage confirms (p99 1.0 to 1.4 s) [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Where a far learner's wait goes"]. Gate 12 (2026-11-14, the adopted B0-29 shape, routing by answer time) missed at API p99 2,011 ms (99.93%); its "own apply" was a split-script artifact: near learners apply at p99 0 to 6 ms, and their reads wait behind the one read-index batch in flight (grant wait p99 0.9 to 1.6 s against round trips of 0.6 to 0.9 s), so a follower or learner now keeps up to `READ_PIPELINE` batches in flight (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "a follower or learner keeps several read-index batches in flight"). Leader memory on that shape: 83 MiB by the analytics peak, the operator's accepted baseline, with a 100 MiB stop line on every gate [measured, `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads-3.md`, "Gate 12 on fdf363f3"]. With it on main 31d19c9, gates 13 and 14 passed (API p99 1,575 and 1,477 ms) and gate 15 missed by 19 ms (2,019 ms) with one learner taking 50.2% of reads; those three ran under the admission throttle that main's pricing fix removed, so the line now needs three passes on current main, with the edge's herding guard ("the edge spreads a learner read over at least three learners") and the send timestamps that split the read-index round trip's wire from the leader's input queue [measured, same, "Gate 15 on main 31d19c9"]. The per-invocation counter on B0-29's shape (2026-11-14) put 77% of the learners' heavy CPU in narrow answer builds, about 2,605 storage reads each: every item loaded before paging, and the named-account scan per state [measured, same, "Instrument run (b)"]. narrow-builds pages lists from `issue_order` keys and keeps the named accounts as a derived index `apply` moves, so a 30-item page at 500 PRs reads 54 to 154 storage statements, against 1,556 to 1,655 (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`narrow-builds` (1)" and "(2)"); its gate 17 is next. **Leader memory under B0-33's load (2026-11-24)** [measured, `tests/am1/results/archive-memory.md`]: on g3A and g4B the leader climbed to 124.8 and 114.8 MiB, then reset, with each step at an alarm-step log walk that fetched about 55 to 66 MB of stored rows in one page. `log-page` (merged `11f1a69a`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`log-page`") sizes each page by its rows' lengths to at most 4 MiB. Hosted on lpB (graft-m2b, main `11f1a69a`, 5,000 agents, 300 s): leader peak 81.6 MiB against memguard's 120, `memSplit.logPageHigh` at most 4,191,755 bytes on every member, 0 wasm resets, 0 leader changes. The leader still climbs steadily (7 to 81.6 MiB over the 300 s) with memSplit's counted parts flat; the per-invocation read ties the first 61 MiB to the peak of open requests (r 0.90). `open-bytes` (merged `b7d3f54b`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`open-bytes`") counts the request and answer body bytes held open, with their peak beside the open-request peak, in `memSplit.open`. Read on B0-33 clA (main `daa4dcc2`, 2026-11-24) [measured, `tests/am1/results/archive-memory.md`, "The open-bytes read on B0-33 clA"]: **open.high does not explain the climb**. The leader peaked at 89.1 MiB with `open.high` at 4.2 MiB and every counted part under 3 MiB, and memory rose on for 100 s after `open.high` and `requestsHigh` stopped. The climb follows entries passing through the log and the leader's traffic, so it is uncounted live heap or fragmentation [inference]. `heap-count` (branch `heap-count`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`heap-count`") reports `memSplit.heapLive` and `heapPeak` beside `wasmMemMb` to tell them apart. Read on B0-33 ln (900 s, main `801d63ad`, 2026-11-24) [measured, `tests/am1/results/archive-memory.md`, "The heap reading on B0-33 ln"]: **heapLive plateaus on every member**, so no leak and no growing fragmentation. The leader's wasm memory held 90.9 MiB for the last 10 of 15 load minutes at heapPeak 58.8 and heapLive 36 to 45 MiB, the followers 63.3 and 73.6 at heapLive about 18, and wasm sits 16 to 32 MiB of allocator slack above the live peak. A 246.5 MB archive scan moved nothing, and no `Archived` committed in the row. At ln's rate (81.5 commands a second against clA's 117), the open part is about 22 MiB of stable live heap only the leader holds that memSplit does not count. **Census (2026-11-24, $0)** [measured, same, "The census of the leader's uncounted heap"]: the leader-only uncounted live heap after load is 27.5 MiB (the 22 compared heapLive with heapLive, and the followers' 5.9 MiB of dump caches is counted). 19.4 MiB of it is test-faults' BF-14 arrival ring: `arrival=mem` keeps one `serde_json::Value` row per `/commit` received, 509 B each on wasm32, up to `ARRIVALS_MEM_MAX` 40,000, and on B0-33 only the leader receives `/commit`s. The leader's uncounted heap less the followers' rose at 555 B a `/commit` and stopped once about 40,000 had arrived, at about 13:51Z, where heapLive stopped rising. Product builds have no ring. Of the 8.1 MiB left, about 3 MiB is the host's leader-side `Stats` sample arrays [inference] and about 5 MiB is not attributed. A native leader sim puts the core's leader-only bytes under load at about 3.9 MB of resolved tickets, which OUTCOME_KEEP frees, and nothing in the core that grows with the stored log or the open-PR set (F115) |
| F66 | What keeps a leader's `InstallSnapshot` source still long enough for a follower to load it? Each archive run moves the source, and an install that finishes against an older source is refused ("install refused: the loaded fold is not at through"). Under LF-13's 1,024 B test archive cap the leader archived about once a second with no commits, and the follower stayed `installing` for minutes [measured, `tests/am1/results/lf13-m3c.md`, "Driver changes"]. In production `LOG_MAX_BYTES` and `SUM_EVERY_MS` (60 s) make that rare, but nothing ties how fast the source moves to how long an install takes [inference] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, `InstallSnapshot`; `build/BUILD-PLAN.md`, LF-13 | Catch-up of a far or slow follower on a hot group whose log archives often, and learners joining one | M3-WP5's install path: hold the source a follower is loading until it finishes or a bound passes, or accept a newer source mid-load, with a test that archives during a load |

| F70 | Found in `admission-stored-bytes`: should the leader's cut count an issue command's `render` against `ENTRY_MAX`? `ENTRY_MAX` (1.5 MB) bounds one entry against the 2 MB row limit and, with `append_batch`, the bytes one AppendEntries carries, but the cut (`Admission::cut`, `crates/raft/src/member/leader.rs`) adds each command's whole `encoded_len`, including the reply render, about 10 KB of the owner's AuthzFold as hex per issue command [measured, LM-1 round 15b], which no event stores and no follower receives. B0-33's entries held 7.6 commands, so neither binds there and this costs nothing measured yet [inference]. Counting stored bytes against `ENTRY_MAX` too would need COMMAND_MAX to stay on the encoded length, since the request body still carries the render. `leader-render` (2026-11-20, branch `lm1-core-rows`) answers it the other way: an issue command's first send no longer carries the owner's `AuthzFold`, so its `encoded_len` falls by the fold's hex (a test asserts at least that much on a small fold, `crates/api/tests/task_api.rs`, `a_leader_rendered_reply_is_the_edge_rendered_reply`), to about 550 B plus the rest of the render, and `ENTRY_MAX` should no longer bind before `ENTRY_SOFT` on a backlog of issue commands [inference, not yet measured hosted]. A command sent again after a missed receipt still carries the whole render, so a retried send is priced as before | `design/DESIGN.md`, Scale ("Entries per group"); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`admission-stored-bytes`" | The cut of large issue-command batches; the bound on bytes held in a leader's queue | No gate row. | F72 | Found in `memory-budgets`: should a member's `confirms` drop a storage confirm that never ends? The host returns before `confirm_end` when `sync()` fails or the member is dying (`workers/site/src/host.rs`, the confirm task), and `Member::confirm_end` keeps an unended confirm forever. A dying member resets, so only a failed `sync()` without a reset leaks, about 40 bytes per input. An unended confirm also excuses every later missed deadline from `commit_errors` (`own_stall`), so pruning it changes `STEP_DOWN`, not only memory | `crates/raft/src/member/health.rs`, `confirm_end` and `own_stall` | `STEP_DOWN` on a leader whose storage hung; a small leak | No gate row. Settle with a sim of a `sync()` that fails without a reset |

A ticket refused `NeedIdBlock` and re-sent after the lease loop records a block maps old to new in `Core::reissued` (`workers/site/src/host.rs`), and nothing removes it, because an edge may still ask `outcome` by the old ticket. It grows by one entry per such refusal, which happens only when a group runs out of ids, so it is small in every run measured [inference] | `workers/site/src/host.rs`, `reissued` and `current` | Host memory on a group that leases id blocks often | No gate row. | F74 | Found in `admitted-bytes`: what makes a leader's member calls miss CALL_DEADLINE under sustained load? On B0-31 m3fr5c, repo:65566's leader at 04:33:41 to 04:33:46 saw 3 to 9 calls in each burst miss, and Workers Logs show the callees answered every one in 0 to 200 ms of their own wall time: the calls were sent 2 to 13 s before they ran on the callee, and the answers to calls sent across 7 s arrived in the same millisecond on the leader (170 of 222 late answers in instants shared with 3 or more peers) [measured, `tests/m0/results/admitted-bytes.md`, "The churn follow-up"]. So the time is spent before the callee's handler starts, on the caller's side or in the platform's queue, not in the callee's work. Answered in part by that trace [measured, `tests/m0/results/read-rounds.md`]: the calls waited behind thousands of open calls on both ends, and those came from a forced round to all 10 targets per read on a lapsed or suspect lease, 250 to 560 to each target in one millisecond, each answered by a learner with its own call; `read-rounds` sends one round per clock reading, to the voters (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`read-rounds`"). L0-12 m3fr6 (build a304d3fc, with read-rounds) still sent the voters bursts of up to 99 read rounds per voter in one millisecond, because lease-clock's floor gives each read in a turn a new clock reading; `read-round-gap` caps rounds at one per READ_ROUND_GAP while the last is unanswered (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`read-round-gap`"). Next: what stalls a busy member's isolate for seconds? On m3fr6, sites 0 and 1's 1 s ticker woke 1 s or more late 31 and 23 times (91 s and 67 s in all, worst 11.2 s), and fast calls arrived with stamps 2.8 to 3.0 s ahead of the frozen clock, so lagMax there measures the receiver's own stalls, not stamps delayed in transit [measured, `tests/m0/results/read-round-gap.md`]. A second mechanism, without stalls: on B0-30's rerun (graft-m1 repo:1048590, site day 9 from cb24cb47), during two sustained archive runs the leader's calls missed CALL_DEADLINE 456 of 3,057 and 715 of 3,742 times, against 60 of 1,911 between runs. Caller and callee loop lag stayed 0 at p99 and follower work p50 was 112 to 120 ms on the misses. The legs grew with the leader's open `/raft` calls (misses 11 to 12% at 0 to 5 awaiting, 29 to 32% at 9 or more), and calls to both followers missed in the same second 66 and 92 times against about 24 and 36 if independent [measured, B0-30 rerun thread's calls.py rows]. Not yet named: whether the archive's own R2 and shard fetches share the six-connection limit on fetches waiting for headers (`research/cloudflare.md`) with `/raft` calls; `awaiting` counts only `/raft` fetches [inference]. B0-33 round 29 (m37, read-round-gap deployed; `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, "Round 29"): voter-to-voter fetches per 20 ms fell from 427 to 562 to 39 to 92, and a read's round left within 120 ms of its arrival for about 95% of grants that waited, so the gap's added wait holds; grants of 1 s or more were round legs, not the gap. The legs are endpoint-bound: per-5 s leg medians correlate 0.80 between calls sharing a callee and 0.76 sharing a caller, against 0.20 sharing neither, and handler work stays p50 0 ms. The voters' inbound was about 330 requests a second each, of which `/suspect` was 29 to 40% (30,039 / 29,892 / 42,087 in 303 s, sent by the edge: 41,497 edge requests, each reporting again on each retry) and `/read` 49 to 56% [measured, graft-m2b Workers Logs 06:30:42 to 06:35:45Z; the inbound-trace branch's write-up]. Next: `inbound-trace`'s `Entered` rows on LM-1's next B0-33 rerun say how much of each out leg is client load that entered the callee ahead of the call [inference until then]. B0-33 m39, the inbound trace's first hosted run: client load ahead at the callee is refuted, since a delayed call entered a callee with 3 handlers open (p50) and arrivals during its leg ran at the callee's ordinary 333 to 374 a second. The legs are held on the caller, in its six-slot fan-out per invocation: legs p50 1,000 ms for calls 1 to 6 of a turn, 1,434 ms for 7 to 12, 2,693 ms for 13 or more, and 46 of 3,418 calls past the sixth answered before a slot could free [measured, `tests/m0/results/fanout-dups.md`]. What fills the fan-out is duplicate forced appends to one voter in one turn (1,146 of site 0's 1,245 appends in turns of 10 or more calls repeat a last index already queued); in the simulator every repeat comes from `read_round`, whose slot the floored member clock frees again within a turn [measured, scratch count], and `fanout-dups` times that slot on the isolate's own clock, one round per voter per turn (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`fanout-dups`"); that the hosted floor jumps that far is [inference]. B0-33 m41, with `fanout-dups`: duplicate appends are gone (1 of 26 repeats in turns of 10 or more calls), and 78% of voter-to-voter calls leave in turns of six or fewer, yet legs ran p50 513 ms and p90 3.4 s. Confirms and loop stalls on both ends overlap 71% of the leg time of calls with legs of 1 s or more, against 42% for the same calls shifted 7 s. An out leg sent with the caller's confirm in flight ran p50 3,076 ms against 959 ms. Confirm time rose with inbound, and after the first step-down voter inbound rose from 140 to 330 req/s to 650 to 740, mostly edge `/read` and `/suspect`, with misses rising from 21% to 71% of calls [measured, `tests/m0/results/m41-held.md`]. Which edge path sends the extra `/read` is open, and the `ReadQuery` trace (26) names it on the next B0-33. `lookup-share` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`) makes an isolate run one leader lookup per chain and one `suspect` per member and term per SUSPECT_GAP. Two candidates are held for after that run: STEP_DOWN's own-stall exclusion covers only a confirm begun before the send, so 1,148 m41 misses held by the caller's own later confirm still counted; and the leader's transaction size (100 to 659 statements a flush in m41's first 30 s), which tripped the first Confirm step-down at p99 721 ms. Open until LM-1's B0-33 rerun shows the voters' calls in time |
| F75 | Found in `lease-clock`: can a read lease still serve past its end when every member is saturated at once? The member clock is floored by the newest peer or edge stamp, and while a stamp of the last 8 arrived `DRIFT` / 2 or more past that member clock (`clock-guard`; it was past the isolate's own clock until 2026-11-18) the leader grants reads only by a round sent after their arrival. A read can still wait in a busy leader's queue after the edge stamped it, and if both followers are saturated too, no fresh stamp reaches the leader, so its clock and the guard both read stale together. The read would be served past the lease only if that common lag passes the 1.5 s margin between a lease's end and a follower's campaign (`ELECTION_AFTER` 10 s against `LEASE` 9 s less `DRIFT`), a new leader is elected and a write acknowledged in that window [inference; no stale read measured]. What would measure it: guard fallbacks per minute (`/stats` `clock.leaseFallbacks`), the lag distribution per member (`clock.lagBuckets`, `lagMax`), and any stale read in a probe that saturates all three members while it writes and reads one key | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, `DRIFT` and the read path; `tests/am1/results/lease-clock.md`, "What the branch changes" | Read-your-writes on lease reads under a whole-group overload | The `lease-clock` site day's lag and fallback counts per member on B0-31, B0-32 and L0-12; a saturating stale-read probe if lags reach the margin |
| F76 | Found in BF-54: how does a `Repo`-scope runner get a match whose answer was lost, and why was a held 50 s poll answered after 100 s? With 2,500 listeners on one broker (`BROKER_HELD_MAX` 2,000), 277 of 310 matches were still `Assigned` 45 s or more after they committed (p50 368 s), and 70 jobs started more than 60 s after their match [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, BF-54]. **Answered [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "F76"]:** (1) The shard's `match` gave a runner that holds an unfinished job nothing, and `Pick::Resend` after `RESEND_AFTER` existed only on BF-55's `Owner`-scope path. So a lost `Repo`-scope answer waited out the 10-minute `JOB_LEASE`. (2) The lost answers were not a slow leader. The broker's isolate died. From 21:09:20Z, the first job minute, `broker:actions:7100001.11` on site a ended `exceededMemory` 14 times, each failing 618 to 1,836 held polls at once (21 times across sites a to c as the edge moved polls). Its peak memory rose from 47 to 109 MiB while holding with no jobs to 256 to 270 MiB. Each death dropped every held poll, with any match it had just committed. The edge's poll then waited out the runner's 100 s timeout: 84 to 346 edge polls a minute ended at 100 s from 21:10Z, against none before. The leader was not the limit. Its member used 7 to 18 s of CPU a minute (12 to 30% of a thread) and served `/runsvc` at p50 1 to 17 ms before jobs and 84 to 1,298 ms after. Asks were about 290 to 430 a second during the hold, which fits 2,000 polls asking every `ASK_EVERY`. The cause of the memory is an inference: every hint woke all 2,000 polls, each ask held its request and the lookup's future, and once answers took 0.3 to 1.3 s, ten times as many asks were in flight. **Fixed on branch `f76-repo-resend`:** `ActionsFold::repo_pick` runs `Taken::pick`, the `Owner`-scope rule, over the one shard, so a lost `Repo`-scope match is resent after `RESEND_AFTER` (`crates/actions/tests/gates.rs`, `a_repo_scope_runner_whose_match_answer_was_lost_gets_the_job_on_a_later_poll`, which fails on main's rule with "no answer after 2 minutes: Busy"). A broker also has at most `ASKS_MAX` (64 [guess]) asks in flight, so a hint no longer starts 2,000 at once | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions runs and jobs" (Label matching); `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Identity", `ASKS_MAX`) | BF-54's job start p99 of 35 s and its 60 s fail line; BF-55's lost-answer rule | **Settled [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "BF-54 run 2"]** by BF-54's rerun on site day 46 (main `cab0b332`): 0 matches `Assigned` 45 s or more (run 1: 277), 0 broker `exceededMemory` (run 1: 21), broker peak 69 MiB (run 1: 270 MiB), and no edge poll at 100 s. BF-54 still fails on job start, for F77's reasons |
| F77 | Found in BF-54 run 2: why does a `Repo`-scope job start late, or never, once its match reaches its runner? Of 512 jobs, 511 started at p99 39.0 s after their run committed (gate 35 s), job 1185242 at 62.0 s, and job 1185331 never ran a step: it was acquired (`InProgress`) 14.4 s after its run committed, its runner never ran it, and it failed at its 10-minute lease end [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "BF-54 run 2"]. Two causes are measured, the second only in part. (1) The edge read the repository's secret rows before each `acquirejob` and answered 503 on 74 of 586 tries ("secrets: the repository's rows did not answer", `workers/edge/src/actions.rs`, `job_config`), and the runner waits 15 s before each retry: job 1185242 took four tries. (2) An acquire that commits but whose answer the runner never gets: the edge's `acquirejob` took up to 24 s, its only 409 of the run came in the same 20 s as job 1185331's acquire, and the runner's filtered log cannot show which call was its own [inference, as run 1's job 1182509]. What a fix needs: the secrets read must not fail an acquire that the shard could answer (read it beside the commit, or retry it within the call), and a second `acquirejob` by the same runner for a job it holds `InProgress` must answer the job message again rather than 409 (the `Acquired` transition's idempotent twin of F76's `Resend`). **Answered [measured, `tests/m4/raw/wp4/f77`]:** (1) All 74 failures were on `repo:65565`, the run repository's group, which every acquire of the run reads. None was a missed deadline or a failed lookup: in each failure's trace the edge asked two or three members, and every voter answered 200 within 1 s (p50 233 ms, max 898 ms, CPU at most 22 ms); 36 failures also asked the removed ex-voter `#1#f1`, whose answers took up to 3.1 s. The edge read the whole tiers (`{"rows": true, "packed": true}`), and a voter sheds that read, as `overloaded`, when its tiers changed within `DUMP_EVERY_MS` (1 s) of its last dump rebuild, which the run's check-run commits did every few hundred ms. The edge's 503 dropped the lookup's error, so which answer each member gave is an inference from the code, confirmed live on m4a: a follower's whole-tier read after a tier change shed 4 of 6 times (`retryAfterMs` 190 to 589) while its access read answered every time (`shed-repro.mjs`). (2) The runner retries `GetJobMessageAsync` up to 5 times with a 5 to 15 s back-off on any error but 404, 409, and 422, and on those logs "Skipping message Job" and drops the job (actions/runner v2.338.2, `src/Runner.Common/RunServer.cs` `GetJobMessageAsync`, `src/Runner.Common/RunnerService.cs` `RetryRequest`, `src/Runner.Listener/Runner.cs` lines 735 to 745). **Fixed on branch `f77-acquire`:** `job_config` reads the narrow `{"rows": {"actions": true}}` (`TierRows::actions_rows`: `settings` and every `actions_secret` and `actions_variable` row), which no dump spacing or `HEAVY_READS` sheds and a follower serves with its metadata tier loading; a member built before it answers every row, and a read that fails still fails the acquire (`workers/site/src/fanout.rs`, `the_acquire_secrets_read_is_never_shed_for_a_dump_rebuild`, which fails on main: main's members answer the query with the whole tiers). `Acquired` on a job `InProgress` is its own state, so the holder's repeated acquire writes nothing and gets the message again with the same lease, and the shard still refuses any other runner (`crates/actions/tests/gates.rs`, `a_runners_repeated_acquire_of_its_own_job_is_answered_and_anothers_is_refused`, which fails on main with "Acquired is not a transition from InProgress") | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions" (Runner protocol, Who decrypts), "Actions runs and jobs" (Leases); `workers/edge/src/actions.rs` | BF-54's job start p99 of 35 s and its 60 s fail line; any job whose acquire answer is lost, which waits out `JOB_LEASE` and fails | **Settled [measured, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "BF-54 run 3"]** by BF-54 run 3 on site day 47 and edge day 21 (main `1d90863a`): 512 of 512 jobs succeeded, job start p99 16.6 s after the run committed (run 2: 39.0 s), max 19.2 s, none unstarted after 60 s; 512 `acquirejob` answers, all 200, 0 `job_config` failures (run 2: 74). (b) was not exercised hosted, since no acquire answer was lost and no runner asked twice (0 repeated acquires, 0 409); it rests on `crates/actions/tests/gates.rs` and plants 25 to 27 |
| F78 | Found in R24's hosted run (run 1966233): how does a hosted runner's delete see a job it holds on another of the owner's shards? `DELETE .../hosted/runners/{id}` commits `DeleteRunner` on `OWNER_SCOPE_SHARD`, and `hosted::may_delete` refuses it only while a job on that shard names the runner. A mark now carries its first sighting, and a read counts it only when it is older than its own shard reads' start. | F92 | Deferred from the Confirm StepDown trace (2026-11-18): should a leader whose own storage confirms tripped `STEP_DOWN`'s confirm rule judge a handoff target by the target's own confirms, not by its answer times? `peer_storage_slow` (`crates/raft/src/member/health.rs`) calls a follower slow when `commit_fail_min` of its answers in the window took `confirm_p99_ms` longer than its fastest, so a follower whose `sync()`s are as slow but whose answers are not yet stretched passes, and the leadership moves to storage no better. On B0-33 m51 (4 learners) site a's leader tripped at 11:17:42.318Z on 16 inputs, 5 over 500 ms, from syncs of 0.7 to 3.7 s; the new leader then had slow syncs 6.6 a minute against the old one's 0.3; on m50 (8 learners) 9.4 a minute against 0, and m50 changed leader twice. An idle m51 follower had syncs of 2.4 to 4.4 s, so the stalls are the platform's, as in F35 [measured, `tests/am1/results/health-gate-lease.md`, "The Confirm StepDown"]. The candidate rule: the follower reports its own confirm samples (or a slow count) on its append answers, and the leader hands off only to a target under the rule's own threshold; a trip with no such target holds the leadership. The sim's `slow_storage_everywhere_keeps_one_leader` and `slow_storage_on_both_followers_keeps_the_leader` must keep passing  **A third run, and the cause tested (2026-11-18).** B0-33 m52 (site day m52, 4 learners): site a's leader had 36 of 320 syncs at 1 s or more (max 4,144 ms) and handed off on its 14th confirm alarm at 14:48:08.8Z; site b then had 12 of 71 at 1 s or more (max 3,806 ms), its first 7 s after the transfer, while the other members stayed under 150 ms [measured, `tests/am1/results/b033-m52.md`]. Across m50, m51 and m52 (`tests/fanout/syncload.py`, per member and second, `raw/syncload-m5{0,1,2}b33.txt`): no follower had a sync of 1 s or more under load (about 1,800 follower-seconds), while leaders had 3.0 to 12.8 a minute; a leader second at 0 to 25 inbound req/s still had 0 to 6.3 a minute against 0 for followers at the same rate; within a role the Spearman of a second's sync-busy time against its inbound is −0.04 to 0.13 for leaders and 0.10 to 0.26 for followers (pooled 0.51 to 0.59, which is the role difference). What a leader does that a follower does not is write large transactions: its deferred flushes run p50 98 to 311 statements against 2, and the share of its syncs at 1 s or more rises with the statements confirmed (m52 site b 0% under 250, 60% at 1,000 or more; m50 site b 1% and 19%) [measured]. So the evidence favours the leadership's own writes over storage luck and over inbound request rate [inference]: luck predicts slow follower syncs at a comparable rate, and none appeared in three runs. It also means the candidate rule above would not stop the churn: a follower's own confirms are fast because it is a follower, so every target passes, and its syncs slow within 3 to 7 s of leading (m51, m52). The candidate becomes: a confirm trip under write load holds, as a trip with no target already does, rather than transfer **Correction (2026-11-18, `tests/am1/results/tx-mix.md`).** A follower applies the same entries and writes the same fold statements, lease rewrites included, but at once rather than deferred, so `Flushed` never traced them: its "2 statements" was a tracing artifact. On the same indexes followers confirmed m52's 1,000+-statement transactions in p50 24 ms (max 85) against the leader's p50 545 ms (max 2,825), and m51's in p50 18 against 50 [measured, `tests/fanout/txmix.py`, `raw/txmix-m5{0,1,2}b33.txt`]. So statement count multiplies something only the leader has (its `arrival` writes at 40 to 47/s against 14 to 21, event-loop lag, reads, inbound) rather than causing the slow sync alone [inference]. The leader's largest transactions are `ExtendLeases` rewriting every live lease (about 1,365 statements on m52, 46% of its deferred statements), fired by stalls of a few seconds; test-faults `dup` is 13 to 19%, and `arrival` 31 to 44 implicit transactions per m52 leader confirm. tx-mix's cuts (`arrival=mem` and an in-memory `dup`; an `ExtendLeases` floor) are the next test of this row | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape (`STEP_DOWN`); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "six slow confirmations" (its flip) | B0-33's zero-leader-change line; every load row whose leader hands off on platform stalls | Deferred until the learner work lowers leader load (orchestrator, 2026-11-18). A B0-33 run on the shed-backoff build with 4 learners: if the leader still changes on confirm trips with the target's syncs as slow, this needs its own `crates/raft` design, sweep and Porcupine |
| F93 | Found building M4-WP3 slice B (`gh-token`): which `default_workflow_permissions` should a job get, and how long should its token live when the job sets `timeout-minutes` over 360? GHES has a repository, org and enterprise setting, "Read and write permissions" or "Read repository contents and packages permissions", that sets the default before a workflow's `permissions:` [doc: GHES 3.22, "Managing GitHub Actions settings for a repository", Workflow permissions]. Graftyard has neither the setting nor its REST route, so every job without `permissions:` gets the restricted default (`graftyard_actions::token::default_permissions`). The token lives `JOB_LIMIT` (360 minutes) plus a lease whatever the job's `timeout-minutes`, so a self-hosted job set to run longer gets a 401 from the API after about 6 h 10 min, under GHES's 24 h | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions", `GITHUB_TOKEN`; `crates/actions/src/token.rs` | Workflows that rely on a write default, or that run past 6 h and call the API | No gate row. The setting is a repository row plus an `Authz` org policy field and `GET/PUT .../actions/permissions/workflow`; the lifetime can read the job's `timeout-minutes` from its spec, capped at `JOB_TOKEN_MAX` |

| F99 | Found in B0-29 m3fhg (2026-11-19): what should a leader do when one of its own storage confirms stays open for seconds while its event loop runs? On m3fhg one `sync()` on the site 0 leader took 23,957 ms. The output gate held every send and reply behind it, the isolate kept taking requests with 0 loop-lag rows, and no AppendEntries failed before it. The followers elected site 1 about 14 s after the last send (`ELECTION_AFTER` 10 s plus the election), and `/stats` commit p99 went to 1,325 ms [measured, `tests/m0/results/held-grant-m3f.md`, section 1]. Today the followers' election is the only exit. `STEP_DOWN`'s confirm rule judges a confirm only when it ends, and any transfer it started would wait at the same gate. Candidates, weighed with no code yet: (a) a leader whose oldest unconfirmed write is older than a bound (2–3 s [guess]) while its loop is alive stops admitting, or steps down, from the member's own clock. Its sends wait at the gate, so the step-down reaches the followers only after the flush, and stopping admission is the part that acts at once [inference]. (b) Followers pre-vote sooner when the leader's AppendEntries and promise renewals stop, below `ELECTION_AFTER`, which trades this stall against more leader changes on m0-wp9's 1–2 s stalls. What decides it is how often each class happens per hour of load. The kept traces (`tests/fanout/raw/tsave-*`; each voter's confirm rows grouped into 10 s events, logged when they end) give, per hour of load, stalls on a member that led when its confirm row was read: B0-29 (12 traces, 3.85 h) 0.5–2 s 10.7/h and 2–10 s 8.6/h; B0-33 (23 traces, 4.69 h) 32.6/h and 22.8/h; B0-31 and B0-32 (7 traces, 1.57 h) 1.3/h and 1.3/h. Over every voter, stalls of 10 s or more were 2 in 10.1 h: m3fhg's (begun on the leader, read after it lost the lead) and one on a follower in B0-33 `m36r33` (11.6 s) [measured; the 10 s grouping and the read-time leader are inferences]. A bound that fires on the 2–10 s class would move leaders several times an hour, and one at 10 s gains little over the election **Recounted 2026-11-24 [measured, `tests/fanout/f99stalls.py`], on 105 kept traces, 25.48 h:** F99's method as above (the leader at read time, 10 s events taking their largest confirm, hours as each trace's confirm-row span, setup included). Rows are parsed by `json` as exact integers, never through float, and deduplicated only on the exact row with its site, so heavyturns' rounding (LM-1) cannot merge two (847,804 confirm rows either way). Leader stalls per hour, 0.5–2 s / 2–10 s, with the hours each family gives: B0-29 11.4 / 4.6 (25 traces, 7.98 h); B0-31 20.7 / 1.9 (4, 1.06 h); B0-32 7.7 / 3.6 (15, 2.20 h); B0-33 42.4 / 20.1 (53, 9.41 h); B0-35 18.9 / 4.1 (8, 4.83 h); all 24.3 / 10.0. The 2–10 s class is mostly short: of 256, 197 were 2–3 s, 48 were 3–5 s and 11 were 5–10 s, all 11 on B0-33. Stalls of 10 s or more: 3 in 25.48 h on any voter, the 2 before plus lcA2: m3fhg (24.0 s, B0-29), m36r33 site 2 (11.6 s, a follower, B0-33) and lcA2 site 0 (10.3 s on a leader, B0-33, syncs.py's verdict `storage`: the isolate ran 4,683 inputs while it was pending). So about 1 per 8.5 h of hot-group load, 1 on a leader in 25 h by the read-time rule (2 counting m3fhg, which began on it). Of the 15 leader stalls of 5 s or more (a leader row within 10 s of the event; 14 of them B0-33), all but one (m45b33) had a leader change between the stall's start and 20 s after its end (`terms-TAG.jsonl`), so the election already ends them. **Candidate (b)** (followers pre-vote sooner): a follower's silences from its leader of 5 s or more, with no hole in its own trace, ran 61.7 an hour over all traces and 35.5 an hour at 9 s; only 18 at 5 s and 12 at 9 s overlapped a leader confirm of 2 s or more. On b9m3f4 none of its 12 fell inside the load window, so most are setup and idle time [measured on one trace; inference for the rest]. A pre-vote at 5 s would have fired on about 87 times as many silences as it would have saved, and below `ELECTION_AFTER` it adds leader changes in every quiet minute [inference: the count of silences is measured; that each would have started an election is not]. **Candidate (a)** (stop admitting past a bound): the only option that acts at once, since its sends wait at the gate. A bound at 5 s would have fired 12 to 15 times in 25.5 h (0.5 to 0.6 an hour, by the read-time rule or a leader row within 10 s), every one on B0-33 but m3fhg, and stopping admission there costs no leader change. **The counts favour (a) at a 5 s bound, and neither is worth building now:** one B0-29 row in 25 traces met the case, and the election ended it. What would change that: a leader stall of 10 s or more on a second B0-29, B0-31 or B0-32 trace (11.2 h of their traces so far), or a 5–10 s leader stall outside B0-33. (b) would win only if a follower's silences of 5 s or more outside a leader stall fell to about the rate of the stalls themselves, under 1 an hour | `tests/m0/results/held-grant-m3f.md`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`STEP_DOWN`'s confirm rule needs six slow confirmations, and `INLINE_MAX_HOT` is 64 KiB (2026-10-30)"; `design/DESIGN.md`, Scale, "Commit on `Tri`" (the output gate) | B0-29's commit line (one stall fails the row), B0-33's zero-leader-change line, and B0-06's gray-storage leader | No work package. Recounted at $0 on 2026-11-24 (above); (a) at a 5 s bound is the candidate if a second 10 s leader stall lands on a B0-29, B0-31 or B0-32 trace, and no hosted row is scheduled |

Cause of the walk's length found [measured, `tests/m4/raw/wp4/b035-m58/lookup-mark-t4.txt`]: the record's superseded bootstrap name `#1#f1` ended every pass "no leader", and the pass forgot f0, which both followers named, after one miss. | F104 | Found in B0-35 m59's analysis (2026-11-20): why did site a's git shard catch-up of one entry with no packs (3529) take 21 s, `heads` 1,875 ms then 19 s before its page read reached any member, while the same isolate's lookup mark for `repo:65573` was held by 13 walks in a row (00:01:34.9 to 00:01:55.4Z), each starting with no cached leader? Every read and demand naming a later commit waited on it, and it is the whole of m59's dispatch tail: without it the site's p99 is 7.1 s, against 17.3 s [measured, Workers Logs; inference, `tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`, "Deeper analysis of m59"]. Which call drops the cached leader before each walk (`commit_as` on `done.notLeader`, or `learn_leader` on a reply with no `meta.term`) is not traced. **Estimate from the code and the trace (2026-11-20, `catchup-local`):** none dropped it before 12 of the 13 walks. With no cached leader, `stage` posts f0 first under the mark, and the trace would show it; t15109's second pass, after its pass forgot f0, does exactly that ("post 0/… Cached Walk"). Every other walk's first traced post is f1. A request gets to f1 without the mark only after it posted f0 as the cached leader, which takes no mark and was not traced, and only after that post missed or answered `busy`, then missed again after LEADER_RETRY. Each pass ending "leader kept" needs f0 to have counted exactly one miss in the pass, and only that second post counts one: no answer within CALL (3.5 s), or a 5xx [read from the code, `graftyard_lookup::lookup`, `keep_named_leader`]. The open part is why this isolate's posts to f0 missed while the walks' own posts to f0 answered in 95 to 955 ms. The 262 `Sync` `/commit`s from `/demand` (`commit_as`) are the likely walkers [inference]. The walks run one at a time because a waiter whose `tried` holds the cached leader never takes the cache's answer (`found` filters it out), so it waits for the mark and walks f1 and f2 again only to find f0. A waiter that sees another walk end with the leader it tried kept could ask that leader again once instead; that would cut each wait to about one f0 round, about 1 s [guess; not built]. The new lines (a cached leader's miss with its answer and time, and each mark wait with its holders) show the miss's kind on the next row **m60 (2026-11-20):** every walk began with that request's own cached-leader post missing, none with a dropped cache: 125 posts from site a's shard isolate to f0 missed in 2 to 98 ms while f0's object ran a 2,652 ms CPU learner `/raft` and a 2,288 ms CPU alarm [measured]. Not a reused stub: each post builds its own [read from the code]. `lookup-miss-kind` logs the thrown error and its flags, and waits for an object that threw `overloaded` instead of walking. The waiter idea above (a waiter whose holder found the leader asks it once) is recorded only, not built: on m60 f0 could not answer anyone for about 5 s, so it would not have helped. **f0's CPU (2026-11-20, step-agree branch):** the two long invocations were a learner `/raft` from 198 (a read-index batch, 2,652 ms CPU, 05:23:23.784 to 26.522Z) and f0's alarm (2,288 ms CPU, 05:23:27.121 to 29.583Z) [measured, Workers Logs]. On graft-m1, `authz:7100001`'s site-1 voter was replaced in a `Degraded` repair: `#1#f1` answers every read `notLeader` with `meta.removed` and its own last config (211, `Degraded`, lost site 1), while the group's site-1 voter is `#3#f1` in config 212 [measured, `/read` with `index`, 2026-11-23]. A cold isolate holds no config, so `spread_once` asks the record's bootstrap names; F1 answered `nl` first on 9 of cw2's 24 cold isolates, costing 160 to 572 ms each before F2 served [measured, `tests/coldwrite/hosted/raw/cw2.jsonl`]. Nothing the edge holds before its first read names config 212, and the removed member cannot name it either, which is why `cold-write` cw3's lever 2' was not built (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`cold-write`", cw3). Two options, neither built: a removed member answers a distinct "removed" refusal that the pass skips at once, which costs the edge nothing new but is a site change and ships only through a site day; or the edge takes the group's config from the `Release` view or the `MemberRegistry` rows, which is a new read on the cold path. **B0-32 adds a warm case (2026-11-23):** on m3fk2B the removed site-1 voter `repo:65624#1#f1` still named the leader, so it took read-index reads it could never apply and shed each one `overloaded` after FOLLOWER_WAIT, with no config in the answer. Without it the line was 496 of 500, not 447 [measured; the recount is an inference, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Rows m3fk and m3fk2"]. The first option, applied to read-index reads as well, covers both cases. **Warm case answered by `removed-read` (2026-11-23, built at $0, ships on the F113 and G39 site day):** a replaced voter learns it was removed from its site's registry row and its replacement's `/status`, because it never commits the config that drops it, and then answers every read-index read at once `notLeader` with that config and `meta.removed` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`removed-read`"). The cold case's first try, a removed member answering `notLeader` naming none, is unchanged. Owner: B0-32's thread for the warm case; F112's thread for the rest | `crates/lookup/src/lib.rs`, `spread_once` and `learn`; `workers/site/src/member.rs`, `not_leader_reply` | Every cold isolate's first read of a group whose voter a repair replaced, for as long as the removed member answers | The warm case: the next B0-32 row on graft-m3f after the site day, on a replaced-voter group, where the leader's `ReadIndex` count from the removed member falls and the line is judged on 450. The cold case: a B0-34 row on a group with a repaired voter, counting first-try `nl` answers from the removed name before and after one of the two options |

After that, the first dispatch after each idle gap of 3 to 4 min answered 503, at 07:40:17, 07:44:25 and 07:48:26, and its retry about 5 s later answered 201. Each 503 read "the repository's tier rows did not answer; retry (repo:65596: every member declined a read the leader never serves: overloaded (tierOff))", so every member asked declined the `{"rows": true}` read and one named a leader (`Spread::TierOff`) [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`, "HostedRunnersAppKey, end to end: row r1"]. At 07:55Z all three voters were followers at term 2 with no leader, f0 holding `ledTerm` 2. **Cause found and fixed in code, not hosted (2026-11-25, `elect-again`).** It is not tier loading: a member restored from storage has its metadata tier loaded at once (`Repo::load_fold`), since each input writes both tiers in one transaction, so the first hypothesis here was wrong. Every member of an idle group restored from storage is a follower that knows no leader, and an idle leader sends nothing, so each answers a read-index read `notLeader` naming none, and the edge's verdict is `Elect`, not `TierOff`; the two shared one reason, `(tierOff)`. Its probe made the member that led resume (f0 held `ledTerm` 2 at 07:55Z) and taught the followers the leader, but the read was shed anyway, so only the client's retry was served [inference from the code; the raft half measured natively, `an_idle_group_restored_whole_resumes_its_leader_on_one_report`]. The edge now asks the members once more after a probe that finds a leader, within LOOKUP_FOR, and sheds `(elect)` only on a second decline (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`tier-off-leader`", the `elect-again` amendment). **Still open: the first window, 07:34:21 to 07:39:00Z,** five 503s instead of one. Site b's lookup marks show reads at 07:34:21 and 07:37:35 going to `#1#f1`, which a placement replaced with `#2#f1` at 07:30:09.8Z, and following its config (index 1, `removed`), so that isolate's lookup seems to have held a config older than the replace [inference]. What settles it: the per-member line `elect-again` adds (`spread_line`: each member asked, its answer's term, leader, config index and `removed`, and the config index the isolate held) on the next idle-repository read after a replace. The driver's 2 s dispatch retry (`tests/m5/a17/hrak.mjs`) masks this F row  | `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`tier-off-leader`" (Decision, edge half) and its `tier-off-elect` amendment; `design/DESIGN.md`, "Follower read" | GHES compatibility for any client that calls a quiet repository and treats a 503 as a failure (`gh workflow run`, Octokit without retry); every hosted row that creates a repository and acts on it at once | No work package: the fix ships on the next graft-m4a edge day after its merge (M4-WP4's for BF-70 r2, or LM-1's for BF-71). A relying party whose clock lags the edge's by any amount refuses a token minted that second, and one with no skew allowance refuses it for as long as it lags [inference] | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions", OIDC claims; `crates/actions/src/oidc.rs`, `claims` | Every relying party with a clock behind Cloudflare's, such as a self-hosted Vault or MinIO | The offset is the lead's ruling (2026-11-25): 600 s, GitHub's, over a value under the token's 300 s life, because a backdated `nbf` cannot make a token usable before its mint (it is signed at `iat`) and `exp` is unchanged, so it widens nothing after the mint, and matching GitHub's published tokens is the safer compatibility choice for relying parties tuned to them. Settled by the hosted run after `oidc-nbf`'s edge day (BF-71's): a job's token taken by a relying party whose clock lags the edge's by a minute.
