The product half stays open: the first customer install with its own host still owes its setup's before and after. **BF-74's row was built at $0 (2026-11-25):** `tests/m4/wp3/bf74/row.sh` serves graft-m4a on `m4a.graftyard.dev` with the install's `HOST` on it, so `iss` names it, then runs the check, the rule, the check again, and `rp.py` (a stdlib-only relying party with urllib's defaults, judged by `rp_test.py` and plant250); plan and driver list in `tests/m4/results/m4-wp3-bf73-bf74.md`. **Built on branch `oidc-bic` (2026-11-25), not applied to any zone:** `graftyard oidc bic-rule` puts one configuration rule (`ref` `graftyard-oidc-bic`, `set_config` `bic: false`, expression the install's host and `/_services/token/` only) in the zone's `http_config_settings` entrypoint, keeps every other rule, rewrites a stale one in place, writes nothing when it is already there, and reads it back; `graftyard oidc check` fetches the JWKS as `Python-urllib/3.12` and fails on anything but 200 (`cli/src/oidc.rs`; DESIGN, "Install operations", Tools; plant244). Zones that have the rule: none (graftyard.com and graftyard.dev read no `http_config_settings` entrypoint, 10003, 2026-11-25). **Platform fact [doc]: an install whose `HOST` is on `workers.dev` can never pass the Python-urllib JWKS fetch.** Every `workers.dev` request goes through Browser Integrity Check on Cloudflare's own zone: "You cannot turn off this check for a `workers.dev` hostname", and a zone's configuration rule "does not affect the check on the `workers.dev` zone" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/routing/workers-dev/). So `bic-rule` refuses a `workers.dev` host with that reason before any API call, and `check` names it as the cause instead of only the 403 (on graft-m4a, `graft-m4a-edge.<sub>.workers.dev`, 2026-11-25: exit 1 with that reason). By the lead's ruling (2026-11-25) no rule is written into graftyard.com or graftyard.dev, which serve no install, and graft-m4a takes none. **It reaches customers [measured, 2026-11-25]:** a product install's issuer is `https://HOST/_services/token` on the install's own host (`workers/edge/src/actions/oidc.rs`, `oidc::issuer` of `c.cfg.host`; `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions"), a custom domain in the install's zone, not workers.dev. Both of the vendor's zones, `graftyard.com` and `graftyard.dev`, have Browser Integrity Check `on` (the zone setting `browser_check`, read through the API), Cloudflare's default ("Browser Integrity Check is enabled by default", https://developers.cloudflare.com/waf/tools/browser-integrity-check/ [doc]), and `https://graftyard.com/` answers `Python-urllib/3.14` and `libwww-perl/6.72` with 403 "error code: 1010" while `Go-http-client`, `Java`, `aws-sdk-go-v2`, `Vault`, `node`, curl and an empty agent get 200. So a customer's relying party on a stock Python or Perl client is refused the discovery document and the JWKS on any install in a zone with the default, which GHES never does. Found by BF-70 r2 on graft-m4a (2026-11-25): a relying party fetching the issuer's documents with Python's `urllib` and its default user agent (`Python-urllib/3.x`) gets 403 "error code: 1010" from workers.dev, before the edge runs; `curl`, `python-requests`, `Go-http-client` and any other agent get 200 [measured, `GET .../_services/token/.well-known/jwks` with each `User-Agent`, 09:28Z]. 1010 is Cloudflare's browser-signature ban, a zone security setting, not the edge's code [inference]. The row's own verify step now names its agent (`tests/m4/wp3/oidc/hosted.yml`) | `tests/m4/raw/wp3/oidc-hosted/r2/job-before-1/sts.log` | A relying party on a stock Python client against any install: a custom-domain install until its setup runs `bic-rule`, a workers.dev install always; GHES has no such block | Owed by the first install with its own host: its setup runs `graftyard oidc bic-rule`, and its result records `graftyard oidc check` as Python-urllib before (403, 1010) and after (200), and the zone it wrote. F130 stays open until then. Not a code change in the edge, which never sees the refused request; the issuer's two documents are public and static, so BIC protects nothing there |
| F131 | **Built on branch `bf75-artifacts-rest` (2026-11-25), hosted by BF-75:** Found building `actions-artifacts` (2026-11-25): Graftyard serves no REST `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/artifacts`, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/runs/{run_id}/artifacts`, artifact zip download, or delete, so `gh run download` and the web UI's artifact list do not work, though v3's in-job upload and download do. GHES 3.22 serves all of them [doc: GHES 3.22 REST, "Artifacts"] | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions", Artifacts; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`actions-artifacts`" (its Flip) | A user or tool listing or downloading a run's artifacts outside the job; per-artifact `retention-days` and the expiry REST reports | M4-WP3 once these routes enter the oracle: fold rows written at finalize, listed by REST, the zip composed from the stored chunks |
| F132 | Found building BF-72 (2026-11-25): a push or pull request run whose workflow calls a reusable workflow in another repository is skipped by `ActionsTrigger`, which reads only its own network's git cache shard; only the edge's dispatch resolves another repository (`api::read_as`, anonymous, as the launch service does) and reads its file. A `./` call runs from every trigger. The trigger would need the same name lookup and a read of the other network's shard from the repository leader's alarm [inference] | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions", Workflow engine; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`reusable`" | A pushed or pull request workflow that `uses:` another repository's workflow, which GHES runs | M4-WP3 when a gate row triggers such a run from a push: the trigger reads `Names` and `Authz` for the called repository and its network's shard. `on: push` is being built now for BF-78 (M4 parity, 2026-11-25), so the push trigger's plan into another repository is what this row waits on |

## Cloudflare platform facts

| Id | Question | Stated in | What depends on it | Settled by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | Do a Durable Object's storage followers span regions? Answered no by M0-WP11 (`tests/m0/results/wp11.md`, (b)): Cloudflare puts the five followers in "four other data centers nearby" (https://blog.cloudflare.com/sqlite-in-durable-objects) [doc], and `ctx.storage.sync()` after a write took 2 to 37 ms per object across 60 objects on six hints, under the 73 ms of the shortest cross-region member pair in B0-12, so the 3-of-5 confirming quorum is inside the object's region [measured] | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; "Synthesis decision", flip condition 2 | Flip condition 2, which does not fire: `Tri` stays the base for every install. `Solo`'s RPO for a region is not covered | M0-WP11, done 2026-10-27. Reopen if a later measurement sees confirmation slower than the nearest region pair |
| C2 | Does a Durable Object call through a binding avoid the proxy path that failed on December 5, 2025? Mostly answered by M0-WP11 (`tests/m0/results/wp11.md`, (a)): a binding call reached objects in MIA, MRS, and SIN with only the caller's own header and no `cf` object, while the caller's public request carried `cf-ray`, `cf-connecting-ip`, and `x-forwarded-proto` [measured]. The November 18, 2025 postmortem lists every impacted service, including Workers KV through its proxy-fronted gateway, and not Durable Objects; the December 5 impact was limited to FL1 zones with the Managed Ruleset [doc]. The transport between colos is undocumented [unverified] | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; Shape, "Members reach each other through Durable Object bindings" | The default's partial-proxy claim, and the case for one account over account-separated sites | M0-WP11, done 2026-10-27 as far as research and measurement reach. No test can reproduce a fault inside Cloudflare's proxy |
| C3 | Can every site script, the edge, and the Workflows script bind the member classes of all active, spare, and residency sites in cycles with `script_name`, and does a deploy of one script leave the other scripts' bindings working during its rollout [unverified]? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | `ReplaceMember` onto a spare with no new script version, and every site day | M0-WP1 builds the cyclic bindings and M0-WP4 (B0-04) deploys under load [inference: no row names this question]. M6-WP4 (BM-6) requires that no script version is created. Partly settled for the Workflows script (M6-WP1, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp1.md`, "C3"): it is its own script binding the site classes by `script_name` [doc], so its deploy creates no site-script version when the spare is in `sites.json` from genesis; graft-m6a's BF-50 is the hosted check. The rollout half stays open |
| C4 | Is 30 ms the right floor for a voter pair's round trip, a floor that separates regions only if intra-region round trips stay under it [guess]? M0-WP1 measured 150 pairs of members with one hint on one site (`tests/m0/results/b0-12.json`): pairs on one colo all stayed under 30 ms, but 9 of 50 enam, 8 of 50 weur, and 34 of 50 apac pairs reached 30 ms or more, because one hint spreads members over many colos (apac over SIN, NRT, HKG, KIX, and ICN) [measured]. The floor separates `Tri`'s continents and does not separate two voters inside one hint region | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; Shape, `MIN_PAIR_RTT` | `Placement::new` refusing misplaced voters | M0-WP1 (B0-12, plus the intra-region measurement) |
| C5 | Does the R2 get-bucket `location` field report actual placement or only the hint? M0-WP1 (`tests/m0/results/b0-13.json` and `b0-13-edges.json`): for 120 buckets, 20 per hint, the API returned the hint in upper case for every bucket, and read latency from probes in every region put all 120 on the hint's continent. Between neighbouring regions, 76 of 80 enam, wnam, weur, and eeur buckets were nearest a colo that hint's probes land in; the other 4 were within 3 ms of one [measured]. Latency cannot split enam from wnam, or weur from eeur, more finely than that | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | `graft-ops` refusing a misplaced bucket at install | M0-WP1 (B0-13) |
| C6 | Where does a Durable Object restart after a whole-region failure, and does a recovering old leader ever run beside a new instance of itself? Answered by M0-WP11 (`tests/m0/results/wp11.md`, (c)): a replacement starts only after 3 of 5 followers agree to stop confirming the old instance's writes, and an old instance whose event touches storage gets an exception, so an old leader may still run but cannot commit [doc]. The followers sit in the object's region (C1), so after a whole-region loss the object cannot restart until its region returns [inference]. "Dynamic relocation of existing Durable Objects is planned for the future" [doc] | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | `Solo`'s region RTO (the region's outage) and RPO (writes not yet in object storage, batched every 10 s). The protocol tolerates both cases | M0-WP11, done 2026-10-27. No gate row, because fault injection cannot reproduce Cloudflare's behavior |
| C7 | Does Durable Object storage still depend on a third-party provider (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 23)? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | All three sites share Cloudflare's DO storage layer, so a correlated failure under all of them stays possible. The design mitigates June 2025's class but does not remove it | No work package. Accepted risk |
| C8 | Do new container instances start during a control-plane outage [unknown]? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | Cold networks fetching after warm shards' one-day reserve runs out | M0-WP6 (B0-19), which the operator skipped on 2026-10-29 (`FRAME.md`). The control-plane row stays `contained`, and warm shards stay sized to carry a day |
| C9 | Does native git over SSH work on Pro with no client `cloudflared`? **Answered yes, hosted end to end on 2026-11-20.** History: on 2026-10-31 the Spectrum API refused a Tunnel virtual-network origin on Pro (10012, 13002), so SSH became a `cloudflared access ssh` option on Pro and Business and Spectrum-to-Tunnel on Enterprise (B0-24b, never run); the option ended between the lines over 24 h, 99.587% of 28,347 sessions with 143 kills and no acknowledged push lost [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24a]. **B0-24c (2026-11-19):** a Pro zone created a TCP Spectrum app on port 22 with a Worker origin, whose `connect()` took raw TCP both ways and held 600 s with 1 MiB byte-exact [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24c]. **B0-24d (2026-11-20):** a Worker's call to a Durable Object's `connect()`, piped to the object's container port 22, echoed 1 MiB byte-exact in 18 ms after open and 4 MiB in 839 to 991 ms, open 20 to 137 ms warm and about 2 s cold, and binding `props` reached `ctx.props` while a public request saw `{}` [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]. The operator then made SSH native through that path, off by default per install; the `cloudflared` option, Tunnel, and B0-24b retire (`FRAME.md`, "Operator decision, 2026-11-19, native SSH on Pro"). The hosted end to end on `graft-m3f` passed: stock clone, push, a key refused after removal, and a session held 633 s [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]. Open: B0-24a's 99.9% session gate with kills is not yet run on this ingress; Business is the same by the plan table [unverified] and Enterprise [inference] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Stack (Spectrum), Shape ("SSH front"), journey 4, "Milestone 0" (SSH row), "Compared with the AWS design" (SSH row), "Tradeoffs accepted", "Open questions and risks"; `alternatives/AWS.md`, condition 5 | SSH on every plan, M6-WP2, and `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "SSH goes through a Spectrum Worker application to the SSH front (2026-11-19)" | M6-WP2: the B0-24d hosted row and B0-24a's gate on the Spectrum ingress. Business and Enterprise: the first install on each plan |
| C10 | Do the 100 MB, 200 MB, and 5 GB request body limits apply to chunked `git push` bodies? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | Each plan's stated push cap and the edge's own cap | M0-WP6 (B0-21, which sets each plan's stated cap). On workers.dev, yes: chunked bodies are cut at 100 MB like sized ones (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`). On the Pro zone `graftyard.dev`, yes too [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`]. Business and Enterprise are not run and stay [doc], owned by the first install on each plan |
| C11 | Will Cloudflare raise Container, Workflow, and vCPU limits per account for a managed cell, and do its terms allow one service to span several accounts? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | Managed cells and the account-separated option | No gate row. Needed before M7-WP2 and M7-WP3 [inference] |
| C12 | Will Secrets Store, a beta product on the installation-token path, stay up for longer than an isolate's one-hour key cache? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; Stack (Secrets Store) | A longer failure stops Actions job starts and App tokens install-wide [inference]. It touches installation-token minting in M3-WP4, `graft-ops` tokens in M5-WP3, and `graft-mcp` sealing (A11) | No work package. Accepted risk |
| C13 | Will Code Orange's progressive config and cohort rollouts (`research/cloudflare-uptime.md` finding 25) hold? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | Nothing in the design depends on it. If they hold, the provider-wide rows marked exposed happen less often | No work package |
| C14 | Which Workflows concurrency figure binds, 10,000 or 50,000, when A1 needs 500? **Moot (2026-11-25):** the controller runs a `TaskBox` Durable Object per task, not a Workflow (DECISIONS "`controller-taskbox`"), and objects have no account-wide count limit | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Open questions and risks" (`research/agents.md` 6.5) | Nothing now | None |
| C15 | From a third-walk report: does FTS5 work in Durable Object SQLite, given that `research/cloudflare.md` cites FTS5 only for D1? Answered [measured, 2026-11-13]: yes. A hosted probe created contentless FTS5 tables with `contentless_delete=1`, and porter, unicode61, and trigram tokenizers, ran bm25, `snippet`, prefix, phrase, `optimize`, and `integrity-check`, rolled back a transaction, and kept 5,000 documents across an object reset, both with no jurisdiction (DFW) and under `eu` (WAW). `sqlite_version()` and `PRAGMA compile_options` are refused with SQLITE_AUTH, so the version is not known (`tests/m4/c15/`) | `design/DESIGN.md`, Stack (Durable Objects) and Shape (`SearchShard`) | Cross-repository search in `SearchShard` (M4-WP2, BF-68) and FTS5 in member folds (M2-WP1) | No gate row. M2-WP1 builds FTS5 in member SQLite first and finds out there [inference]. The writer asked that Milestone 0 confirm it |
| C16 | From a third-walk report: does one hook owner's `HookSequencer`, a single object under the 1,000 requests a second soft limit per object, need a split by hook hash for a very large org? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Failure modes" (`event pipeline`, noisy-tenant) and Shape (`HookSequencer`) | Webhook throughput for the largest owners | No gate row measures one owner's ceiling. M4-WP1 (BF-57) runs 50 hooks at 10 events/s |
| C17 | From a third-walk report: does a `workers.dev` route work as `graft-runners`' webhook URL, or does the runner account need its own zone? | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape (`graft-runners`); `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions" (Hosted runners) | The `workflow_job` webhook that prompts a demand read. The 5-minute sweep works either way | No gate row. M4-WP4 builds the route |
| C18 | From a third-walk report: which CPU architecture do Sandboxes run, and can arm or larger hosted labels be offered [guess: Linux x64 only]? | `design/SHARED.md`, "Actions" (Hosted runners, label table) | The hosted label table | No gate row. M4-WP4 builds the label table |
| C19 | Found in M0-WP1: does a `durable_object`-policy container keep starting in its object's region? The `default` policy is refused on this account and will not be offered (operator, 2026-10-27; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Git cache shards run under the `durable_object` scheduling policy"), and `durable_object` takes no `regions` constraint. Milestone 0 measured 90 of 90 starts in the hinted region and no restart across a new version [measured, `tests/m0/results/container-placement.json` and `container-survival.json`], but the docs say an object and its container are "not guaranteed to run in the same location" [doc] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Git cache shards (derived)") and "Open questions and risks" | Each site carrying its own networks after a region loss, and the start check that replaces a misplaced container | M0-WP1 (B0-38), rerun at M1-WP5 on real shards |
| C20 | Found in `m0-carried-6`: how does the leader keep its AppendEntries clear of earlier unconfirmed writes, and what keeps a leader's event loop behind on some placements? The output gate holds a send until all previous writes confirm (https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/storage-api/), so a far-follower send issued during the previous entry's confirm waited for it (p50 256 to 342 ms late with over 150 ms left); and 2 of 16 B0-02 gate runs, both with the leader in ORD, had 46 to 53% of the leader's trace on one clamped millisecond phase, commit p99 1,000 and 1,226 ms [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-6.md`] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Scale ("Commit on `Tri`"); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "The leader sends AppendEntries before its own append is durable" | B0-02 (8 of 8 at 300 and at 330) | The send half is closed: every held send was issued in a turn that wrote nothing, and the near follower makes the quorum, so the host keeps its send order (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "The far follower's held sends get no host change"). The loop-lag half is open: the failing runs did the same work per command in fewer, larger entries, so `m0-carried-7` caps an entry at `ENTRY_SOFT` ("An entry stops at `ENTRY_SOFT` bytes past its first command"). The cap removed big-entry heavy turns but not the failures: two same-window A/Bs passed 4 to 7 of 8 per arm with and without it, and the failures were a lagging leader loop under the cap (1 run) and near-follower calls held about 1 s while the follower traced nothing and the leader was idle (4 runs) [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-7.md`]. Both decided by the operator (2026-11-13) and built in `m0-carried-8`: the near-gap traces showed the far voter one call late behind its own outstanding call, not paused, so AppendEntries are pipelined to a voter (`PIPELINE`, "AppendEntries are pipelined to a voter"), and a leader whose confirms read whole clamped ticks steps down (`LOOP_LAG`, "A leader whose loop is behind steps down"). On that code, rebased on main `84d29c2`, the near-gap failures did not recur and no leader was clamped, but the gate passed 12 of 16: two misses were capacity under `ENTRY_SOFT` at 330 (one 60 KB entry per round at a slow near round), one a far follower whose multi-entry calls missed `CALL_DEADLINE` and lost its pipeline, then `STEP_DOWN` to the far site, and one a slow stretch at 300 with no cause found [measured, `tests/m0/results/carried-8.md`]. Settled for B0-02 by `ENTRY_SOFT` 100 KB and `APPEND_BYTES_MAX` 100 KB: the gate passed 16 of 16 on that tree with no rise in heavy turns. Still open: from a weur leader, the apac voter missed `CALL_DEADLINE` on 6 of 32 calls of 45 to 99 KB (r300-3), so the call bound is set for enam's far link only; and LM-1's B0-29 A/B on this tree |
| C21 | Found in B0-33 m57b33 (2026-11-19, graft-m2b, edge f786a952 from main `61853463`): what made a Workers edge isolate trap "memory access out of bounds" inside `worker::Delay`'s timer wake closure (`<worker::delay::Delay as Future>::poll::{closure#0}::{closure#0}`, `wasm_bindgen::convert::closures::invoke`)? After the trap, worker-build's shim reinstantiated the module, every closure of the old instance threw "Cannot invoke closure from previous WASM instance" (394), their requests' promises never completed (199), and the runtime cancelled them as hung (245), in bursts on one 10 ms tick (09:52:04.61 ×35, 09:52:49.61 ×37), 441 edge 500s in all between 09:52 and 09:55Z; m56 on the same sites had none [measured, Workers Logs, `tests/am1/results/b033-m57.md`]. The trap means a timer fired into a freed closure, so a `Delay` outlived its future, but the path is unverified: `Delay`'s drop clears its timer, no select or race in `rcache` drops a `Delay` mid-wait (`batched_from` awaits each poll to its end), and `Running`'s drop touches only plain data. A local workerd bed with the batch's shape (2,500 writes on 8 keys, one in four cancelled) did not reproduce it. The build carried `tier-read-batch`, since dropped; `edge-read-batch` (`rcache::batched`, on main since `8c794619`) polls plain data on the same `worker::Delay` and is the code that still shares the mechanism, with no exception on m3fe2 or m56 | `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`tier-read-batch`" (Dropped) and "`edge-read-batch`"; `workers/edge/src/rcache.rs` | Any edge code that polls on `worker::Delay`, `edge-read-batch` first: one trap takes down every request in flight in its isolate | A trap or "previous WASM instance" line on any edge row; a Workers-side repro from branch `tier-read-batch` (76776859) under m57's load |
| C22 | Will Spectrum's Worker origin and the Workers `connect()` handler stay available on Pro? Cloudflare's blog calls the Durable Object and container TCP path private beta (https://blog.cloudflare.com/grpc-workers/) [doc], the protocols-per-plan page still lists TCP as "No" on Pro and Business [doc], and yet the Pro zone `graftyard.dev` accepted a Worker app on port 22 [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24c] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Stack (Spectrum), "Tradeoffs accepted", "Open questions and risks" | All native SSH; HTTPS stays the default and does not depend on it | No work package. Watched; a withdrawal leaves SSH off |
| C23 | How long may one `connect()` invocation hold a socket? It is undocumented. B0-24c held 600 s [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24c], and a clone of a 5 GB network over SSH lasts longer [unverified] | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Tradeoffs accepted" | Long SSH clones and pushes | M6-WP2: the B0-24d hosted row held one session 633 s [measured, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp2.md`]; a session of an hour or more is not run |
| C24 | Is one Worker application per transport per pay-as-you-go zone (11034) [doc] enough? Port 22 becomes the zone's one TCP Worker app, so no other TCP service can share the zone | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Deployment modes" (front door); `research/cloudflare.md`, "Spectrum and SSH" | Any later TCP ingress on the same zone | No work package. Accepted; a second TCP service needs another zone or Enterprise |
| C25 | Found preparing AM2-WP1's hosted half (2026-11-23): do one install's secrets fit under Secrets Store's beta limits of one store and 100 secrets per account [doc, https://developers.cloudflare.com/secrets-store/manage-secrets/; measured, `research/cloudflare.md`, Secrets Store], and what does a managed account holding several installs do? One install keeps its ops keys (the drain and executor seeds `graftyard genesis` stores, `MintWrapKey`, `SecretsWrapKey` slots A and B, the run-service key, the SSH host-key slots A and B, the ops deploy token), nine, and AM2-WP1's four (`MCP_APP_KEY_A`, `INSIGHTS_APP_KEY_A`, `MCP_CLIENT_SECRET_A`, `MCP_SEAL_KEY_A`), so 13, and at most 26 if every one held a rotation's B slot at once [inference from `cli/src/genesis.rs`, `cli/src/apps.rs`, and graft-m4a's store rows]. That fits at most three to seven installs in one account; the test account holds 21 of 100 for six installs after AM2-WP1's hosted step. A managed account with more installs than that does not fit, and neither does a second install in one account as built: `genesis` looks for a store named `graft-ops-<install>` and cannot create a second, so it falls back to `--offline-keys`, and `apps init` needs `--store` and `--secret-prefix` | `design/DESIGN.md`, Stack (Secrets Store); `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Deployment modes", "Enable agents"; `cli/src/apps.rs` | How many installs a managed account can hold, and whether `genesis` and `apps init` need a shared-store mode by default | A Cloudflare answer on GA limits; or the managed cell's design deciding one account per install (C11) |

## Git and storage

| Id | Question | Stated in | What depends on it | Settled by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| G1 | What is the daily working set of git cache shards, which `cost.py` guesses at 30%, 20%, and 10% of git bytes? **Evidence 2026-11-21, graft-m1 network 1048603:** a warm net shard holding one fully fetched network keeps about 0.78 of R2's pack bytes (0.777 to 0.778 at each site), plus about 8% disk over its pack bytes (1.07 to 1.10), at 36 to 37% of a 20 GB disk [measured, `tests/am1/results/residency-1048603.md`]. This does not answer G1. G1 asks what fraction of all git bytes is hot each day across many networks, and one synthetic network fetched whole in a row's last 15 minutes cannot tell that; the design-partner audit export can. So G1 stays open and `cost.py`'s 30/20/10% guesses stay. Notes, not traced: R2 lost about 320 packs and 206 MB a bucket for 1048603 after 2026-11-20T19:20Z; the shards' `used` moved on 2026-11-21 with no load. | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks"; Shape, `SHARDS` | `SHARDS`, container memory, and the git cache line in `scripts/cost.py` | M0-WP6 (B0-14, which sets the shard count) |
| G2 | Is any repository larger than a git cache shard can hold, which is a 20 GB disk with about 15 GB of packs? | `alternatives/AWS.md`, condition 6; `design/DESIGN.md`, "Compared with the AWS design" (Largest repository) | Monorepo owners, and the choice of platform for them | No work package raises the limit. M0-WP6 (B0-22) rehydrated a 5 GB network in 52 to 118 s, so at the slowest copy rate measured, 46 MB/s, a network over about 6 GB misses 120 s and one of 15 GB takes about 5.5 min (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`) |
| G3 | Should BYOC require the Enterprise plan (default: no, with 100 MB HTTPS pushes on Pro, 200 MB on Business, and Enterprise for anything larger)? | `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Four Cloudflare-design questions", question 4; `alternatives/AWS.md`, condition 4 | Push caps per plan and the install preflight against the largest imported pack | The operator decides. M0-WP6 (B0-20, B0-21) measures the caps |
| G4 | From a third-walk report: is a metadata-write 503 window of minutes during a split acceptable, given that seeding the child before the split would shrink it but departs from the rule that the child starts from the snapshot at `at`? | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Chains", `CHILD_GROUP_AT`) | Issue, comment, and label writes on a monorepo during its split | Answered 2026-11-12: no window. The operator vetoed a metadata 503 during a split, and every command commits on the parent, which reads the child's rows narrowly through `deps` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "A metadata split runs in band on the parent leader, keeps every commit on the parent, and reads the child narrowly") |
| G5 | Should the push path store packs fixed (not thin), and how long does a rehydrate's git work take on real history? (Resolved part: a promotion packs the objects of every pack, inline and stored, that its range names, so the promoted pack is self-contained. Promoting only the inline packs failed rehydrate when the range held a thin stored pack, `index-pack: fatal: did not receive expected object`, and the self-contained promotion passed [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp5.md`, BF-18 runs 1 and 2].) Pushed packs are stored as the client sent them, thin against the refs, because the edge tees the bytes to the buckets while the shard validates. Git keeps no thin pack on disk, so every rehydrate and catch-up re-indexes each one with `index-pack --strict --fix-thin`, and a stored `.idx` of a thin pack is unusable [inference from git; `tests/m1/results/m1-wp5.md`, BF-18, which ingested every pack that way]. B0-22's "git's part is under 1 s" had prebuilt indexes and random blobs with no deltas or thin packs, so its git time is optimistic for a real network (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp6.md`, B0-22, "Not tested here"). Storing fixed packs would mean re-uploading the shard's output after validate, against today's tee **Answered [measured, `tests/fanout/raw/rehydrate-131296-m29.json`, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Stored-idx rehydrate"]:** a cold rehydrate of repo:131296 (257 stored pushed packs, 2.62 GB, never promoted) took 1,694 to 2,496 s per shard on m29, 90 to 96% of it `index-pack --fix-thin` at 1.0 to 1.55 MB/s and 53 to 94 s the spool. So pushed packs stay thin, and a promotion now stores the `.idx` of its self-contained pack (`R2Key::Idx`), which a rehydrate checks against the pack and loads instead of `index-pack`, skipping the pushed packs a committed `Promoted` covers (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`stored-fetch`"). Projected at m29's site c rates, a 5 GB network of promoted packs rehydrates in about 90 s with four fetches at once, 258 s one at a time; the multi-stream R2 rate is the unmeasured variable, and B0-34's cold warm measures it | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Packs", `rehydrate`; "Git cache shards", `R2Key::InlineIdx`) | B0-22's 120 s line on real content, `R2Key::Idx` and `R2Key::InlineIdx`, and the start of a newly warm shard | M1-WP6 (B0-22 on thin packs) and B0-34's cold warm on `stored-fetch` |

| G9 | Can promotion keep up with a hot network's pushes? A promotion is `pack-objects` of the range's objects and then a threaded `index-pack` validate, which measured 4.65 s and 6.50 s for 30 thin pushes of git/git (29.2 MB pushed, 57.9 MB promoted) on 4 CPUs: 2.62 MB/s of pushed bytes, CPU-bound [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Promotion rate"]. B0-31's agents pushed about 4 MB/s, so on that load the unpromoted tail grows by about 1.4 MB/s (754 MB over 600 s in `promotion_keeps_up_below_its_rate_and_stays_bounded_above_it`), and a cold rehydrate of such a network re-indexes that tail. Correctness holds; only rehydrate time grows. Shard-streamed promotion lifts the site's memory bound, not this CPU one. Hosted it is slower: B0-34's drain on `graft-m2b` (standard-4 shards, linux history) promoted entries 18 to 234 in 51 promotions, 7,404,855,988 B promoted in 4,489.6 s, 1.65 MB/s of promoted bytes, covering 211 pushed packs of 2,217,077,014 B, 0.49 MB/s of pushed bytes; a promoted pack is 3.3 times the pushed bytes it covers [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 22]. Against B0-31's about 4 MB/s of pushes, the unpromoted tail grows by about 3.5 MB/s on that load [inference from the two rates]. Range length is data, not a lever: 24 steps a promotion pack at 2.47 times the pushes against 3.24 at 6, and `PROMOTE_MAX` stays because longer ranges lengthen each promotion at the same rate [measured locally, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, round 23b]. `fetch-ahead` drops `--fix-thin`'s bases and names the objects, about 25% fewer promoted bytes and less `pack-objects` work (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`). The next lever is a roll-up of promoted packs as `Compacted { consumed, produced }`: one `repack -a -d` of steps 8..200's 6-step promotions gave 1.04 times the pushes (6.32 GB to 2.18 GB), in 385 s on 4 threads with 8.6 GB resident [measured locally, round 23b]. It would need a memory bound under the shard's 12 GiB beside its other work, such as `--window-memory`, or a geometric roll-up of the newest promoted packs only, and its CPU competes with promotion on the same shard. Stream count is not a lever as `/spool` stands: it answers its headers only after the whole body, so each fetch holds one of an invocation's six connections waiting for headers, and `FETCH_AT_ONCE` 4 leaves the other two to the ingest and the log page. The option, not built: `/spool` answers 200 at once and writes its JSON at the body's end (headers in 2 to 33 ms locally against about 1,000), so more fetches stop counting toward the six. Built only if the per-container R2 ceiling allows more: three cold warms read 104 to 132 MB/s in total with 2.1 to 3.0 of 4 streams busy [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, rounds 24, 26 and 27b] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape ("Packs", `Promoted`); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`stored-fetch`" | B0-34's 120 s rehydrate line on a network pushed faster than promotion runs, and `PROMOTE_AHEAD` | Hosted promotion MB/s beside B0-31's push rate, measured in the B0-34 drain (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`stored-fetch`", flip) |

What would measure it is on main and postdates this run's deploy: disk-validate (merged as `a304d3fc`), where a push validates on the shard's disk as it stands and catches up only for a missing base, so it skips the pushes' catch-up wait; `/procs` on the shard's `/status` (`0c171e6d`, `361a1dc0`), which shows what the container runs; and repack-keep (`0c171e6d`: the repack rolls only the pushed tail, at nice 19 on 2 threads). On close, GitHub keeps `refs/pull/N/head` (it stays after a merge or close, for history) and removes `refs/pull/N/merge`; GitHub's docs call both refs read-only and do not state what happens on close, so this is observed behaviour [unverified for GHES 3.22: GitHub community discussion #11922; the `refs/pull` gist by mtilson; Fluffy and Flakey, "What is a GitHub Pull Request merge branch?"]. `design/SHARED.md`'s pull request section names both refs as derived and says nothing about close. So deleting head refs on close is not an option. | G26 | **Answered [built, branch `g26-head`; hosted check pending]:** the shard now takes HEAD from `default_branch` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "G26: the git cache shard's HEAD is the default branch"). GHES 3.22 documents the default branch as "the initial branch that Git checks out locally when someone clones the repository" [doc, https://docs.github.com/en/enterprise-server@3.22/pull-requests/reference/branches], and github.com's v2 ls-refs names it as HEAD where `refs/heads/main` also exists (axios/axios, default `v1.x`; odysseus-dev/odysseus, default `dev`) [measured, `crates/git/tests/ls_refs/axios-source.ans`, 2026-11-18]. So for a repository whose default branch is not `main`, or which has no `main`, the shard's answers name a HEAD GHES would not: a GHES byte-diff for a client that does not opt in. The edge's v2 ls-refs takes HEAD from `default_branch` since `b034-reads` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`b034-reads`: the edge answers a v2 `ls-refs` from the group's refs, with no shard"). Still affected: the shard's v0 advertisement (`info/refs?service=git-upload-pack` without `version=2`), and every v2 ls-refs the edge sends on to the shard: `peel` over a non-branch prefix (stock `git clone`), a partial page, an unborn HEAD, or a failed read | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Commit and read paths", *Fetch or clone*; `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "GHES compatibility" | The byte-diff invariant for clients of a repository whose default branch is not `main` | Built on `g26-head`: `TestRefsHeadIsTheDefaultBranch` (v2 ls-refs and v0 advertisement on a repository whose default is `dev` while `main` exists; fails on main) and `head_follows_the_default_branch_and_is_named_only_when_it_changes`. It ships with the git image and site scripts on a site day; a hosted v0/v2 check against the edge's answer on the next run closes it |

| G31 | Found in B0-34 mt7 (`tests/am1/results/b034-mt7.md`, "1. The catch-up tail"): should a stored push pack carry an index, so a rehydrate loads it without `index-pack`? Each hot network's rehydrate loaded 8 stored packs with `idx_used` 4 of 8, and its `index_ms`, 10.7 to 22.1 s of a 21.2 to 32.6 s rehydrate, is `index-pack --fix-thin` on the 4 without one [measured, `tests/fanout/raw/shardev-mt7.jsonl.xz`, 22 `shard rehydrate` lines]. Only a promotion stores an `.idx` (`store::put_idx`); the edge places a push's pack with `idx: None`. The push's own `.idx`, which the shard's validate already builds in its quarantine, cannot be stored as it is: a thin pack's index covers the bases `--fix-thin` appended, and an index trimmed to the thin pack's own objects leaves git unable to read a delta whose base is outside the pack ("cannot unpack ... at offset 258" [measured, git 2.56.0]). So the shard would store the fix-thin appendix beside it (`R2Key::Idx`'s pack plus a small `fix` object holding the new count, the appended bases and the new trailer, both create-only and derived), and a rehydrate would write header, thin body, appendix and trailer to the spool and load it as a promotion's pack loads today (`stagedIdx`). Estimated effect: most of each hot network's 10.7 to 22.1 s `index_ms`, so a hot network's cold rehydrate from about 21 to 33 s to about 10 to 12 s [inference: total less `index_ms`, the `idx_used` path's lock time was 0.7 s for 8 packs]. The monorepo's kill-phase rehydrates (`index_ms` 14.8 to 27.7 s over 104 to 131 packs, 60 with an idx) gain the same way. Not in B0-34's gated clone line, whose probes are the monorepo's; it is the hot networks' first clones. Size: a `GET /idx`-style read of the appendix in the container (Go, about 40 lines), the edge's teed placement storing both after validate (about 40 lines in `workers/edge/src/lib.rs` and `crates/git/src/store.rs`), the spooled ingest assembling the fixed pack (about 60 lines of Go), tests of each and a check that a wrong appendix falls back to `index-pack` | `crates/git/src/store.rs`, `put_idx`; `workers/edge/src/lib.rs`, the push's `place_teed`; `containers/git/server/main.go`, `stagedIdx`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`stored-fetch`"; G5 | A hot network's first clones after a shard goes cold (B0-34's second and hostile phases), and any rehydrate of pushed packs no promotion covers yet | No work package. Site b's shard composes"; the switch past the container's 8 MiB `tailMax` is inference, tail sizes are not logged]. Two remedies, neither built: builder failover (the first caught-up shard on the other chain site steps the chain and brings the buckets up while the home's cannot, the home keeping the role whenever it can), which adds a second writer role to the chain; or a larger `tailMax`, which the shard object holds whole | `crates/git/src/host.rs`, `ready_chain`; `workers/site/src/shard.rs`, `Compose::run`; `containers/git/server/compose.go`, `tailMax` | Kill's and second's every-clone share after a home kill longer than a few tip moves | A row where second or kill drops under 90 % on composes past a frozen chain: then failover if the freeze outlasts what a larger `tailMax` covers, else `tailMax`. **Designed (2026-11-23), not built:** the freeze is the restarted builder rebuilding a 260 MB base at nice 19 (mt20b 20:13:54 to 20:15:54, mt14 14:33:58 to 14:38:28) because a new container holds no chain and the builder never installs the buckets' one; proposed fix: the builder installs the buckets' chain before its first step on a container with none, and failover and `tailMax` are not proposed (`tests/am1/results/g37-design.md`). **Built (2026-11-23), not hosted:** A and B on branch `g37-build` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`builder-install`"). Settled by one B0-34 row through a site day: the home's post-restart `warming` window under 30 s, no base build the restart caused, only parts, `head` and the chain's normal folds put to `compose/<net>/` after the kill, and every-clone in kill and second. **Passed hosted on B0-34 mt21 (2026-11-24, `graft-m1`, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt21.md`), the lead's ruling:** no miss waited 2 s on the home's chain after its restart (mt20b 25 over 2.6 min), every-clone 100% in kill and second, and each post-kill base was the fold at the chain's 20th step; the fold was already building at the kill (site 0, 03:14:52 to 03:15:37Z). The install itself is [unverified] there, since the kill fell on a due fold **B0-34 mt24 (2026-11-24, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt24.md`):** the case is open again. The home's builder rehydrated for 330 s, and the buckets' chain got no put from the kill until a fold base at about +375 to +435 s. Site b composed every miss past that frozen tip from +289 to +414 s: p99 15,879 ms, 93 of 111 over 5 s. Every-clone passed beside it (97.7% kill, 100% second), so the flip ("second or kill under 90 %") is met only in part. `builder-install` does not cover it, because the freeze is the rehydrate itself, not a base rebuild after it. About half of the rehydrate is the container's repack, which the rehydrate's own catch-up starts (its `POST /refs` sends no `repack=0`) and the backstop's `/connected` walk then waits out: 164 s on mt24, 99 s on mt21 and 268 s on mt23, whose home rehydrate was 448 s, not 326 [measured times; mechanism from the code]. **The lead's ruling (2026-11-24): G37 is next, at $0 first;** no hosted row until the lead rules on the design. The choice is in `b034-mt24.md`, "G37: the design choice". First take the repack wait out of the rehydrate, with no design change. Then, if composes past a frozen chain remain, the other chain site steps a local chain from the buckets' chain and puts nothing (E), which keeps one writer. Builder failover (C) is still not proposed, because a second writer of `compose/<net>/` needs a fence on `head` **The lead's ruling on the design (2026-11-24):** neither C nor E is built now. C is out: a second writer of `compose/<net>/` with a new fence is a new failure domain, and the rehydrate fix may end the freeze. The rehydrate fix is built on branch `g37-repack0`: the last catch-up's `POST /refs` sends `repack=0` when the backstop follows, so the walk runs first and starts the repack (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`connected-after`", "The last catch-up's ref changes too"). The rehydrate's done line now carries a `walk` step and `stored.repack_wait_ms`. **E is the recorded next step.** Its flip: a row after the `repack=0` fix where composes past a frozen chain still put second or kill under 90 %, or the moved-load site's composed misses read over 5 s at p99 while held. The next B0-34-shaped row (mt24's split, a site day with the new shard image) reads the home rehydrate's wall and its wait, the chain's first post-kill part, and composed against tail on the moved-load site **B0-34 mt25 (2026-11-24, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt25.md`):** the `repack=0` fix holds hosted: the home's rehydrate took 97.2 s (mt24 330), `walk` 12.8 s, `repack_wait_ms` 0. Every clone failed in second at 46.2%, all on `composed` misses past a frozen chain on b (held: composed p99 8,240 ms, 19 over 5 s; `tail` p99 1,432 ms). The freeze is now the fold, which is the chain's 20th step: the home was building the fold base at nice 19 under client CPU 1.0 from about −122 s, so no part was put from 23:21 (about −180 s) to the kill; the restarted home installed the buckets' 19-part chain and built the fold again from scratch before its first put at +116 to +176 s [measured: R2 puts by object name, site 0's `/procs`; the mechanism is read from the code, `handleCompose`'s `len(h.Parts)+1 >= foldEvery`]. E's flip is met. **The lead's ruling (2026-11-25): the fold first, not E,** since E would not have covered the freeze before the kill. The proposed fix (fold aside: the container builds the fold base beside the parts and swaps it in at the next step, with no new writer) is in `b034-mt25.md`, "The plan", for the lead's approval. E stays recorded with its flip. No hosted row until the fix is approved and merged; one more fail on the fold cause stops for analysis **The lead approved fold-aside (2026-11-25), with three additions:** a restarted container deletes a kept fold before its first step, the swap checks that the chain still extends the fold's tip T, and the settling row also reads `chain_head` against `chain_chk` and hits p99 on chains past 20 packs. **Built (2026-11-25), not hosted,** on branch `fold-aside` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`fold-aside`"): `foldStart` 12 and `partsMax` 40, both [guess]. Settled by one B0-34-shaped row on a site day: no gap over 60 s [guess] between part puts outside the home's rehydrate, every clone at least 90% in kill and second, and the moved-load site's composed misses at p99 under 5 s while held. A second fail on the fold cause stops for analysis; E stays unbuilt and C rejected **Answered [measured, B0-34 mt26 on `graft-m1`, 2026-11-25, `tests/am1/results/b034-mt26.md`]: settled by `fold-aside`.** The only gap between part puts over 60 s was 109 s across the kill and the home's 80 s rehydrate; outside it the longest was 54 s. The chain reached 27 parts while its fold built beside the parts and swapped in at 05:26:07. Every clone 99.2% in kill and 100% in second; the moved-load site's one composed miss while held took 1,853 ms. Closed by the lead on 2026-11-25; E stays unbuilt and C rejected |

Its head sat in a run of six capped home pieces of 57 to 62 stored packs each, one log index a piece, 1,081 to 1,513 ms of ingest a piece. Every other merge took at most 1,253 ms (p50 882), against m3fk2B's 15.3, 14.4 and 10.1 s. The home ingested at 18.0 packs a second, against the pools' 14.7 to 20.4. It stayed level with the first pool shard (p50 −0.2 to 0.0 s at every 30 s step, max 0.5 s in load), with `room_calls` 401 over 410 pieces (never two in a piece), `batches` 143 and `adopted` 195 [measured, `tests/fanout/culag.py`, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Row m3fmA"]. What would close B0-32's tail: a merge whose head is in a capped run waits for that run's pieces, not just its own index, so either a merge's catch-up stops at its head's index, or the home's pieces under burst take more than one index each [inference]. **Priced at $0 (2026-11-23, `tests/am1/results/g39-merge-head.md`): neither closes it.** The per-piece merge retry (G34) already ends a merge at the piece that brings its head; m3fmA's merge waited 1,784 ms for the home to apply entries 365 to 386 in order, which no in-order catch-up skips. A run that raises its `until` instead of ending saves about 0.1 to 0.2 s (2,703 → about 2.6 s). Larger pieces save nothing, because burst entries of 57 to 62 packs exceed the 32-pack cap. The lever that would reach under 2 s reads a head from the container's validate aside; it is [unverified] and covers about 1 in 4 merges at k 4. ## Release and operations

It began on 2026-11-13: every run was `ok` with both lines on 11-12 (96 of 96), the first exception came at 10:17Z on 11-13, and the last `ok` at 03:33Z on 11-14, across site versions `0aaedb69` to `17d8d623` [measured, Workers Logs and `workersInvocationsAdaptive`; `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, rounds 21, 22, and 24]. So site a's sweep has not written its cursor or report since then, and neither has its fold audit [inference: both log lines are missing]. | R23 | How does `graftyard runners upgrade`'s 10% canary run the new runner image? A Durable Object-managed container application holds one image, and `graft-runners`' Sandbox classes each have one application, so while the new Worker version serves 10% its Sandboxes start on the old image; the image rolls to both applications only at 100%, a day later [inference: `cli/src/runners.rs`, the Containers applications API]. A bad image therefore reaches every hosted runner at once, outside the canary that `design/DESIGN.md`, "Failure modes" (`actions state`, bad-deploy), asks for. Two fixes: the release registry's record carries a list of images by digest (SHARED, "The registry", has none yet; `runners upgrade` reads its own `images` field today) and the Sandbox classes start a named image per version from a multi-image map, as the git shard's `images` map does; or one container application per image, with the new version's Sandbox classes bound to the new application [inference] **Answered in code [unverified hosted] (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`r23-runnerbox`"):** neither fix as written. Hosted runners start on one `RunnerBox` class on one `durable_object` application with the platform's `ctx.container`, and each `graft-runners` version names its image in its own metadata, so a 10% version starts its own image and nothing rolls. Shipped as releases M1 (add the class), M2 (switch starts, 10% then 100%), and M3 (delete the Sandbox classes after a day with no stale runners) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Failure modes" (`actions state`), Shape (`graft-runners`); `design/SHARED.md`, "Release" (The registry) | The 10% canary of a `graft-runners` release: its image goes to 100% untested | M4-WP4. M1 ran on 2026-11-19, and the cold-burst check passed once the CLI prepared the image (`tests/m4/results/m4-wp4.md`): 8 of 8 `RunnerBox` starts placed on the version's own image, with `run.sh` as PID 1. Open: M2 (10% for a day), and refused placement for want of capacity, not reached |

| R28 | Found checking `purge-outside`'s rejoin rule: can a member name be joined again after it left a group? Two cases. **Learners:** `Placement::new` sets `generation` to the maximum over its current members, while `next_generation`'s doc says a later member "never wakes a removed member's object". After a `/learners` change drops every learner, the generation falls back to the voters' maximum, so a later `/learners` can reuse `#g#l<k>` of a learner that sat in a committed config and kept its log [inference, `crates/types/src/placement.rs`, `Placement::new` and `with_home`]. That log is the group's own, so the leader's AppendEntries conflict check truncates anything that diverged, and a learner counts toward no quorum, so the bug is latent and not unsafe. **Placement tries (by design):** on product builds `Steps::clear` does nothing, so a placement that changes no config leaves its tries joined, and the next one `/join`s the same `#g#f<s>` again. A try never sat in a config and holds no entries (graft-m4a: `config: null`, applied 0) [measured] | `crates/types/src/placement.rs` (`Placement::new`, `next_generation`); `workers/site/src/member.rs` (`replace_steps`, `learners`); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`purge-outside`" | Whether a learner name always means one object's life, which a later rule could rely on | Owner: M4-WP4 ([thread](https://ampcode.com/threads/T-01a1dd0e-a576-7103-ac82-063ed3b27b3c)). Learners: fixed on branch `r28-learner-gen`, code-only, for the next site day. A later placement is built with `Placement::next`, which runs `Placement::new`'s checks and keeps the current placement's generation as the deserialized form does. The site's `/learners`, `/move` and re-placement tries all use it. `a_learner_added_after_every_learner_was_dropped_takes_a_new_generation` fails on main. Tries: by design, no change |
| R29 | Found purging graft-m4a's 65567 (2026-11-18): can a group that commits an entry and then goes quiet leave its followers one entry behind indefinitely? After `DELETE /repos/m3org/b035-r1` committed, `#1#f0` held commit 5044, with `#1#f2` and `#5#f1` at commit 5043 and last 5044. All three were followers in term 26, and none had a leader. `#1#f0`'s lease had ended at 2026-11-17 22:46Z. The followers did not catch up in 4.5 minutes of purge calls, so the gate refused `NoTombstone` on every try [measured]. One `/campaign` to `#1#f0` through the tools edge answered `resumed`, and its next append carried commit 5044 to both. The keep-led timer campaigns only while `F::needs_leader` holds, and a deleted repository with no pending work may not need one [inference, `workers/site/src/host.rs`, `keep_led`] | `workers/site/src/host.rs` (`keep_led`, `alarm`); `crates/git/src/purge.rs` (`gate`) | A purge, or any reader that needs a follower's applied index to reach the group's last commit, on a group with no client traffic after its last write | Owner: M4-WP4 ([thread](https://ampcode.com/threads/T-01a1dd0e-a576-7103-ac82-063ed3b27b3c)). Find why `#1#f0` committed 5044 but neither led nor sent the commit, then decide whether the purge route should wake the group, for example with one read through the lookup before the gate, or whether `keep_led` should count an uncommitted follower as work due. Answered and fixed in code on branch `r29-commit-heartbeat`, not deployed (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`r29-commit-heartbeat`"). Why it sent no commit: a leader sent a follower an append only while it was behind or a read, transfer, or campaign waited, and a follower learns a commit only from an append, so an idle leader never told its followers the last one; this reproduces in memory with no leader loss (`an_idle_leader_tells_its_followers_the_last_commit_once` fails on main f6123990). Why it did not lead: `keep_led` campaigns only while split or archive work is due. Now the leader sends a follower told less than its commit one empty round once the last round to it is `CALL_DEADLINE` old, and the purge route sends one probe read per chain through the lookup first, which starts an election in a leaderless group. Plants 106 to 108 are caught. Open until a site day and an edge day carry it and a purge on a quiet group passes without a `/campaign`. Both now carry it (site days 55 and 56, edge day 28), and scratch repo 65570's purge passed both passes with no `/campaign` and no 503 [measured, 2026-11-19]; its group had a leader, so the leaderless case is still not seen hosted |

| R38 | Found planning BF-39 hosted (2026-11-22): can a site day's 1% step on a thin-traffic site reach `STEP_MIN_ENTRIES` (1,000) at all? A gradual deployment assigns each Durable Object, not each request, a version (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/versions-and-deployments/gradual-deployments/with-durable-objects/ [doc]), so at 1% the N+1 members are about 1% of the site's member objects, and on graft-m4a nearly all of those are idle `Tokens` and `Names` members, which apply almost nothing. `judge_site` then answers `Starved`, and `next` dwells up to twice `STEP_DWELL` and pages, so the day halts with nothing wrong [inference from the assignment rule and graft-m4a's 16 repository chains among thousands of members; not yet seen hosted]. Options, none chosen: count N+1's entries across the whole step's dwell or several steps rather than one window; require a floor of N+1 objects that carry traffic before the 1% step counts, for example by version-keyed probes on a canary group; skip or merge the 1% step below a traffic floor, so the site goes to 10% with the 1% gate's other rules; or a lower `STEP_MIN_ENTRIES` at 1% scaled by the site's measured entry rate. BF-39's hosted row runs with a test-install override (`OPS_STEP_MIN_ENTRIES`, `workers/ops/src/exec.rs`, test-faults only) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Site day (`STEP_MIN_ENTRIES`, `ROLL_STEPS`); `crates/release/src/gate.rs`, `judge_site` | Every site day on a quiet install or site; whether the 1% gate can ever pass without load | A hosted site day with no override on a quiet site, counting N+1's entries per step; then one of the options above. **Seen hosted (2026-11-23, the gate check row, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp4.md`):** with about 50 puts/s on 160 chains homed on c, N+1 applied 0 to 58 entries per window at 1% and 22 to 100 at 10%, while N applied 1,357 to 14,166 in the same windows. No step reached 1,000, so `gate-relative`'s share rule was judged on no step, and the 1% step paged after twice its dwell and passed only on `OPS_STEP_MIN_ENTRIES`. **Row 2 (2026-11-23, about 3,250 c-homed writes/min, 2x row 1):** at 1%, N+1 applied 0 entries for the first 5 minutes, then 71; at 10%, 133 and 256 entries per 60 s window, about 130/min, against N's 6,845 and 13,726; at 50%, 3,141 by 4 minutes. 10% cannot reach 1,000 in a 60 s dwell at any load graft-m4a carries under $8, while production's 2 h `STEP_DWELL` would give about 15,000. **Taken for 10% and 50% (the lead, 2026-11-23):** count N+1 across the whole step's dwell, which `since` already does, and give hosted check rows 10 to 15 minute dwells at 10% and 50%. **10% settled (2026-11-24, gate check row 4, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp4.md`):** with a 12 min dwell at 10% on the real floor (no override) at 1x to 2x load, N+1 passed `STEP_MIN_ENTRIES` 6 min into the step (1,171 entries) and reached 3,973. Every read from then judged the share rule against a real N (R9), at 8 to 5 bp over N's p99, and passed, 8 of 8. **As built, recorded here:** the 100% step has dwell 0, and `ops::next` dwells on `Starved` only when the dwell is over 0, so 100% commits on its first run unless the gate breaches. Row 4's 100% had 878 N+1 entries, under the floor; the first day after it is judged against a real N. **1% stays open:** its N+1 members carry almost no traffic for minutes whatever the load. Row 4's 12 min 1% at 1x to 2x reached 589 entries (0 for the first 5 min) and passed only on the override. **1% answered in code (2026-11-24, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`gate-pool`"), the lead's choice:** a `Starved` 1% step commits after its dwell with no page, and the 10% step counts N+1 from the 1% step's start, so the two steps share one floor; a breach at 1% still runs the `Rollback`. **Closed hosted (2026-11-24, row A, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp4.md`, `raw/ckA/`):** with no override and slow_ms 600 planted on N+1, `SiteDay{c, R11}`'s 1% step committed at `Starved(499)` after its 12 min dwell with no page; 10% counted N+1 from the 1% step's start (618, 737, 867, then 1,068) and breached at 1,068 entries on the share rule, 7,696 bp over N's p99 (1,500 ms) with 0 bp over 5 s; the `Rollback` ran, entries 358 to 363, to `IntentDone` |
| R39 | Found building the vault Worker (2026-11-23): three of DESIGN's vault claims are not what the build does. (a) "noisy-tenant: Pull bandwidth caps per org" (Failure modes, `vault`): nothing caps an org's pull; the vault pulls with the install token, and the edge serves export routes without a per-org limit. **(a) closed 2026-11-24** (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, `vault-turns`): a pass takes owed copies a network at a time from where the last stopped, and DESIGN's row now says the export routes have no per-org cap. (b) "`graftyard vault init` creates the vault Worker ... from the signed artifact" (Install operations, Vault): built in code 2026-11-23, not hosted [unverified]: `vault init` and `vault upgrade` take a release folder and deploy only its `vault/` modules after `check_vault` verifies `release.json` under `Keys.release`; a tampered or unsigned release is refused before any API call. On graft-m4a every version, N and N+1 alike, read a p99 apply lag in the 1,000 to 1,500 ms bucket under load: R3's c members over leg 1's 100% and first day (1,348 to 29,396 entries, 1,112 of the first 1,348 in that bucket), and R3 as N against R4's 1% in leg 3, which breached on the cap with N+1 and N both at 1,500 ms [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp4.md`, "BF-39 hosted"]. Before the row, under the same load, a and b had about 65% of entries in the 1,000 to 1,500 ms bucket and c mostly 150 to 250 ms. A follower on a quiet chain applies an entry only when the leader's next AppendEntries tells it the commit, and R29's `untold` commit heartbeat goes out after `CALL_DEADLINE` (1 s), so a follower applies about 1 s after the leader [inference from `crates/raft`; not traced per entry]. A drained site holds only followers, so every drained day's N+1 reads about 1 s and breaches. Candidate fixes, none chosen: compare N+1 with N on the same role only, measure lag only on entries that were not the last of their round (a later entry carried their commit), or gate on the leader-side commit lag instead of the follower's apply. Answered in code (2026-11-23, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`gate-relative`"): N+1's share of entries over N's p99 on the same site, at most 2%, plus a 5 s backstop at 1%; N at 100% and on the first day is read from the intent's start. Replayed on this row's `gate.txt`, it passes every healthy read and breaches the fault and every plant. Settled in code; the hosted check row, with a real N beside N+1 at 1, 10 and 50%, confirms it |

| R52 | **Answered for the first run [measured, 2026-11-24, the runner account, `tests/am3/raw/r52/`]: a reset neither causes nor speeds a new trigger's first run; propagation alone does.** Three throwaway scripts with a 1-minute cron: one PUT and nothing else fired 5:00 later; the same PUT followed 1:52 later by the LF-7 driver's reset (yearly, then the minute cron) fired 4:43 after the PUT, 2:51 after the reset; a PUT on a script live for 3 minutes fired 9:13 later. A second reset while it was firing skipped no run. LF-7's two agents `graft-ops` deploys fit the same delay: r1 first ran 4:32 after its deploy, r2 10:20 after, so r2's "third reset" 2:02 before its first run did not make it fire [inference from the probe]. Every case is inside Cloudflare's documented 15 minutes. So rows wait instead of resetting: `tests/am3/hosted/cron-fired.py` waits for the first scheduled run in Workers Logs (15 minutes, then one reset and 15 more), and the LF-7 driver's `ops` step stops if none comes. A second instance (2026-11-25, BF-70 r2 on graft-m4a, `tests/m4/raw/wp3/oidc-hosted/r2/`): `graft-m4a-ops` deployed at 09:42:16Z with `* * * * *` did not run within 3 minutes (`fired`); the hand `cron` step (r45-row.sh's reset, then a re-PUT at 09:46:34Z) did not run within 3 minutes either, and the first run came at 09:52:38Z, 6:04 after the re-PUT and 10:22 after the deploy, inside 15 minutes [measured, Workers Logs]. The removal half (runs for up to 8 minutes after a reset to `[]`) is the same documented delay and stays as below. Found again in M5-WP4's rows A and B (2026-11-24, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp4.md`, "Rows A and B"), now a repeat: does a deploy's Cron Trigger fire, and does a removed one stop, on a test install's `graft-ops`? After `node workers/ops/deploy.mjs ... --executor` set `* * * * *`, the trigger did not fire for 3 minutes (row A, 05:56Z to 05:59Z), and one remove and re-add made it fire within 2 minutes (06:00:07Z). The same happened in BF-7, gate check row 4 (two re-adds), BF-46 (`tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`) and BF-47 (`tests/m5/results/m5-wp5-vault.md`). Row B's redeploy over a running cron kept firing. The other way, a reset (a yearly cron, then `[]`) left runs firing for up to 8 minutes in gate check rows 2 and 4 and row B (07:15:54Z and 07:18:28Z resets, last run 07:23:08Z). Cloudflare documents that adding, updating or deleting a Cron Trigger "may take several minutes (up to 15 minutes) to propagate", and "Scheduled invocations can be missed until propagation completes" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/cron-triggers/ [doc]), so both are within the documented delay [inference] | `workers/ops/deploy.mjs`; `tests/am1/results/install-m4a.md`, the runbook | Every hosted row's start and handback on a test install; a production `graft-ops` whose 5-minute cron misses runs after an `OpsRoll` | Not a code question while it stays within 15 minutes. The runbook: after a deploy, confirm the first run in Workers Logs and remove and re-add the trigger if none comes within 2 minutes; at handback, reset and wait for 2 minutes with no run before parking. Settled by a check that times a fresh deploy's first run and a removal's last run on one install, if a row ever needs it sooner |

On a cold isolate `system_record` returns a chain's registry row as its record, with `at: 0` (`RegistryRow::record`, M5-WP2's 7e01f24f). `create` sends that record as each bootstrap member's `/join`. For a half-made system group, with a row on some site but no leader, the joins carry `at: 0`, and since `source-record` every member refuses them, so a cold `commit_system` or `jti` create of such a `Names` or `Tokens` chain fails until a warm isolate creates it [inference from the code]. | R57 | Found by BM-5 clause 3's row m4am1 (2026-11-24, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`, "BM-5's `graft-ops` swap-back, hosted"): should a version upload carry the deployed version's vars instead of the newest uploaded version's? Cloudflare's version upload takes `keep_bindings` "from previous_upload", and an inherited binding defaults to "the most recently uploaded version, which may not be the deployed version" (https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/workers/subresources/scripts/subresources/versions/methods/create [doc]). On graft-m4a, an upload with `plain_text` in `keep_bindings` while eef95142 (11 vars) served 100% carried 13 vars from 3443bb26, an undeployed later upload: clause 1's `FORCE_FORMAT=32.65520` and `FORCE_FORMAT_SITE=0`, and a `FORMATS_ENABLED` with 20.4 and 17.4 that eef95142 lacks. Two such edge versions, 691d05c1 and 2f0a107d, served 100% for about a minute each [measured, `raw/bm5/m4am1/edge.log`, `edge-2.log`]. **Affected:** `graft-ops`'s `Exec::upload` (`workers/ops/src/exec.rs`; `keep_bindings` of every type, so plain-text vars, secrets, services and buckets), which uploads every pinned script's and every site's N+1 under an `OpsRoll`, `EdgeRoll`, `McpRoll` or site day; `graftyard vault upgrade` (`cli/src/vault.rs`, `r2_bucket`, `plain_text`, `secret_text`); `graftyard runners upgrade` (`cli/src/runners.rs`, `secret_text` only, so it re-sends its vars and is affected only for secrets); and test drivers that upload raw versions (`tests/m5/bm5/format.sh`; `upload-edge.mjs` now copies the deployed version's vars). Its runtime settings (`script_runtime`) already come from the deployed version, so only bindings drift [inference from the code]. On a product install every upload goes through `graft-ops`, whose newest version is the one it deployed, unless an earlier upload was left undeployed (a release uploaded and then aborted, or a `Rotate`'s binding change) [inference]. On a test install any ad-hoc `wrangler versions upload` or driver upload sets the vars the next roll ships. DECISIONS M5-WP3's flip ("a version uploaded with `keep_bindings` lacks a binding the deployed version had") names the missing case, not this one | `workers/ops/src/exec.rs`, `upload`; `cli/src/vault.rs`; `cli/src/runners.rs`; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "M5-WP3: `graft-ops` executes intents, as built" | Every N+1 `graft-ops` uploads: a roll can ship a var, a binding, or a format list no one deployed, and its N+1 is then not the release's code plus N's config | **Fix built for `graft-ops` (2026-11-24, branch `keep-deployed-bindings`, not deployed; `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`deployed-bindings`"):** `Exec::upload` reads the deployment's highest-percentage version and sends its bindings as `bindings` (`ops::upload_metadata`), keeping only `secret_text`, whose value no read returns; `r57_an_upload_takes_the_deployed_version_s_bindings_not_the_newest_upload_s` and plant212 (4 of 4). **Secrets, from the docs:** the script version upload endpoint takes an `inherit` binding's `version_id` only as "latest" (10057 otherwise); only the beta `/workers/workers/{worker_id}/versions` endpoints take a version id, and say "use `inherit` bindings for secrets" when copying a version [doc]. Whether `keep_bindings` takes a `secret_text` from the newest upload is not documented, so a `secret_text` may still drift; in this tree only the test edge's `BED_TOKEN` is one under a `graft-ops` upload [inference]. **`graftyard vault upgrade` fixed the same way (branch `vault-deployed-bindings`, not deployed):** it reads the deployment's highest-percentage version (`Cf::version`) and uploads with `ops::upload_metadata`, so its bucket, URL and runtime come from that version and only `EXPORT_TOKEN` is kept; its two mock-API tests assert it, and plant88 #27 and #28 catch it. `graftyard runners upgrade` already sends every non-secret binding from its spec and keeps only `secret_text`, so it carries what `graft-ops` now does. **Settled by** one hosted roll after a stray upload on a test install, reading the new version's vars against N's |
