The code says yes: only the `Repo` family archives, archive objects are deleted only by a purge, and a member that installed a snapshot gets the range from a listing of the record's buckets (`export::archive_holding`, `crates/git/tests/export.rs`, `the_archive_holding_a_cursor_is_found_among_the_chains_keys`). The `release` half is answered [measured, graft-m3f 2026-11-24 16:32:51Z, deployed main `5bed46af`]: a signed install-scope `export/chains/release/entries?from=1` answered 200 with 689 frames from index 1 (58,695 bytes, every frame's SHA-256 checked, no `Link` next), so the oldest system chain is served whole from its genesis. The repository half is not: the only chains with `archive/` objects on graft-m3f are `repo:65577` to `65580` (one object each, from index 1, in sites a's and b's packs buckets, none in c's), and all four were retired on 2026-11-17 (`tests/am1/results/install-m3f.md`, m3fr11). `repo:65577` answered 503 "no learner or follower of repo:65577 answered; retry" twice, 3 minutes apart, because a retired chain has no member to read; no live chain past an archive exists there to read [measured]. A retired chain answered 503 "retry", and the puller did ask for one forever once the feed was stale; the edge now answers a group whose every member is retired 404 and the puller asks a 404 chain no more (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`export-retired`"). **The repository half is answered** [measured, graft-m3f 2026-11-24 21:49Z, BF-53]: `repo:65631` archived 1 to 17 and 18 to 19 into sites a's and b's packs buckets and truncated to 19 while the feed was held; the feed first carried it from 19, and the lifeboat's first copy pulled 1 to 32 from the edge in one page with no 410 and reached every member's commit (`tests/m6/results/bf53.md`). So no snapshot install path is needed | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Lifeboat (the copy starts every chain at entry 1) | Whether a lifeboat enrolled on an old install rebuilds without a snapshot install | Before BF-53: one signed `export/chains/{chain}/entries?from=1` on graft-m3f's oldest system chain and on a repository chain past an archive, each answering 200 from index 1. A 410 sends the snapshot install path back to build. The repository half is settled by BF-53's own row, not a row of its own (the lead, 2026-11-24): BF-53's install runs with a lowered archive cap so at least one live repository chain archives before `lifeboat enroll`, and the lifeboat's first copy of that chain from index 1 is the read. A 410 there fails BF-53 and sends the snapshot install path back to build |
| R60 | Found preparing BF-46's `SshHostKeys` row (2026-11-24): should the SSH host-key secrets carry an install prefix? `workers/site/deploy.mjs` binds `SSH_HOST_KEY_A` and `_B` by those names and `deploy/ssh-front/hostkeys.mjs` writes them, so on the test account, where every install shares one account and one store, a write meant for graft-m4a would replace graft-m3f's host keys [measured: graft-m3f's three sites bind both names in store `bfaa0c15…`, Workers API script settings]. A production install has its own account and store, so it cannot collide [inference]. Renaming now would break graft-m3f's deployed bindings, so `hostkeys.mjs` takes `--sites` and refuses a write while any other script binds the slot's secret in that store (`workers/site/sshguard.mjs`, `refuseForeignHostKey`; `workers/site/tests/sshguard.test.mjs`) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Rotation runbook (`SshHostKeys`) | A second install with SSH on in the test account; managed accounts holding several installs (C25) | A `--secret-prefix` for the host-key slots in both scripts, applied on an install's next SSH site day, or C25 deciding one account per install |
| R61 | Found by BF-53 on graft-m3f (2026-11-24): does workerd close an idle `localDisk` Durable Object's SQLite file? If it does, a lifeboat's open files grow with the chains active at once; if not, with every chain it holds, and the bundle's start floor (4 per chain, at least 65,536 [guess]) must keep pace with the install. BF-53's puller failed every pass with `SQLITE_CANTOPEN` at an inherited 1,024 once about 1,000 of 3,236 chains were open, and ran at 524,288 [measured, `tests/m6/results/bf53.md`]; how many it held open at its peak was not counted | `cli/src/lifeboat.rs`, `start_script` and `open_files_floor`; `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Lifeboat | A lifeboat outage from a limit it outgrows after start | Count `/proc/<workerd>/fd` on a local bundle of a few thousand chains after a full copy, idle for an hour, then after a stale cycle that wakes every copy; or workerd's source for `localDisk` actor eviction |
| R62 | Found by BF-50's row m6aL on graft-m6a (2026-11-25, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp1.md`, "m6aL"): what does a `ReplaceMember` do with a chain that cannot elect without the lost site? On m6aL, after `campaign-rotate`, 3,112 of 3,118 group instances completed and 6 could never finish: `tokens:953`, whose only live joined voter was on site 2 (site 1's member answered 409), `repo:0`, whose placement tries never formed a group, and the first-create leftovers `repo:6201` to `repo:6204`, whose site 1 row was a `Solo` try outside the `Tri` config [measured]. One such group errored the coordinator, so `graft-ops` paged and the `ReplaceMember` stayed current for good: an `Abort` of a `ReplaceMember` moves nothing the executor runs, a `Halt` keeps it current, and a restarted coordinator meets the same group, so `RestorePlacement`, which would let those chains elect again, was refused (`IntentCurrent`) [measured from the code, `crates/release/src/ops.rs`, `steps` and `next`]. Answered by `move-unmoved`: such a group ends unmoved and pages, and the coordinator finishes with it listed. Open: whether `ReplaceMember`'s `IntentDone` should carry the list (a Release format change, not made) | `workers/workflows/move.mjs`, `group` and `coordinate`; `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Site loss | A site loss whose replace never ends, and the restore it blocks | m6aL's recovery half on the fix: the `ReplaceMember` reaches `IntentDone` with those 6 unmoved, restore2 runs, and each unmoved chain elects with site 0 back and loses no acked write |
| R63 | Found by BF-53's feed evidence (2026-11-24): is an hour the right cap on a purge's wait for the change feed (`export::PURGE_FEED_WAIT_MS` [guess])? A purge answers 503 until the feed carried each chain's commit, then retires; past the hour it retires anyway and logs the entries never exported, so a stalled feed never blocks a purge for good. A round is about 15 s [measured, m3fr37], so an hour is about 240 rounds, but a feed held by an operator or a site day may stall longer, and then the vault and every lifeboat miss that repository's last entries, its retire included (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`feed-retired`") | `crates/git/src/export.rs`, `purge_wait`; `workers/edge/src/purge.rs`, `feed_wait` | A purge's last entries reaching the vault and lifeboats; how long an operator's purge can hang on a stalled feed | A hosted purge with the feed held past the cap, reading the 503s, the 200's `unexported`, and the error line; then the longest feed stall graft-m3f's Workers Logs show over a month |
| R64 | Found building `actions-oidc` (2026-11-25): is 24 h the right wait between an `ActionsOidcKey` `Rotate`'s probe and its switch (`ops::OIDC_PUBLISHED_FOR` [guess])? The switch is safe once every relying party that will verify a new token has fetched a JWKS that lists the new slot. AWS IAM, Vault and MinIO each cache the JWKS for a period no document here states, and MinIO read it once at start in the local run [measured: `tests/m4/wp3/oidc/run.sh`]. Both slots stay published, so a short wait fails only tokens signed under the new key at a party with an old JWKS; the old key's tokens keep verifying | `crates/release/src/ops.rs`, `OIDC_PUBLISHED_FOR`; `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Rotation runbook | A relying party that refuses the first tokens after a switch | Each named relying party's JWKS cache time [doc], or BF-70's rotation clause with the wait lowered (`OPS_OIDC_PUBLISHED_MS`), counting refusals after the switch |
| R65 | Found by BF-50's row m6aL on graft-m6a (2026-11-25, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp1.md`, "m6aL"): can a chain's first create and a later placement try produce the same member name, and what stops it? `Member::new` names a member `{chain}#{generation}#{kind}{slot}` with no site (`crates/types/src/placement.rs`). `repo:6201`'s first create placed a `Tri` group with `#1#f0` on site 0, and a later `Solo` try at generation 1, slot 0 named `#1#f0` on site 1 [measured, the registry rows]. They are separate objects, since each site's `Member` namespace is its own, but a registry row, a snapshot keyed by name, or a lookup that drops the site cannot tell them apart; m6aL's snapshot read the `Tri` config before the loss and the `Solo` one after, and judged the chain short [measured] | `crates/types/src/placement.rs`, `Member::new`; `crates/lookup` placement tries; `tests/m6/hosted/bf50-row.sh`, `snapshot` | Every reader that keys a chain's members by name alone | Whether a placement try can reuse a lower generation's name on another site (lookup's try generations, `crates/lookup`), and a test that two tries of one chain never share a name; or the site in the name |
| R66 | Found by BF-50's row m6aL on graft-m6a (2026-11-25, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp1.md`, "m6aL"): should an idle `Tri` chain whose leader's site is lost elect without being asked? DESIGN intends it not to: "Campaigns are request-driven, so an idle group stays led from A until a request comes, and that request waits" (`design/DESIGN.md`, "Downtime", the site-loss walk; Shape, `Member::campaign`, "never started by `tick`"). So a `ReplaceMember` needs the coordinator's `/campaign`: on m6aL 3,114 of 3,115 group instances ran at least one campaign step (3,733 steps; 3,112 chains elected), 1.006 of the 3,095 chains judged held on A [measured, `tests/m6/raw/m6a/m6aL/judge.jsonl`]. What a client sees meanwhile: its first write to such a chain waits for the promise to lapse, then the edge's `suspect` reports and an election, at most about 11.5 s plus its commit [inference, DESIGN's bound]; BM-1 measured 16.8 s for the first push and 5.4 to 6.3 s after [measured, `tests/m1/results/m1-wp8.md`]. m6aL measured none: its load stopped before the fault, as BF-50's before-snapshot requires, so no client write reached such a chain. A restore meets the same: m6aL's `RestorePlacement` started 3,118 groups and none stepped in 8 minutes, since the groups the replace had moved were idle with lapsed promises and the glue asked for a campaign only on a dead replace (fixed by `restore-campaign`, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`move-unmoved`") | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Downtime" and Shape, `Member::campaign`; `workers/workflows/move.mjs`, `group` | A site loss's first-write latency on every idle chain, and every move's need to ask | A row that writes to idle chains led from A after the fault and before their group's campaign, timing the first write; or a decision that a lost site's groups campaign on `SiteLost`. **Measured next by** m6aL2's `probe` (`tests/m6/hosted/bf50-probe.mjs`, ruled 2026-11-25): `gh api` and `git ls-remote` on a real repository homed on A and idle through the loss and the restore, timed from the client, cold and again |
| R67 | Found by BF-50's row m6aL on graft-m6a (2026-11-25, `tests/m6/results/m6-wp1.md`, "m6aL"): should a move that ends a site's role in a chain release that site's warm git shard? Before each leader step onto the spare, `move_step` warms the target's network shard with a client catch-up (`workers/site/src/member.rs`, `shard_warm`), and a client call keeps a shard warm for `WARM_FOR` (24 h), renewed by `INACTIVITY` (6 h) (`crates/git/src/shard.rs`). After m6aL's `ReplaceMember` 25 containers stayed active on the spare (3 on site b, 0 on a and c) with no load, and `graft-m6a-spare-wnam-gitshard` cost $2.86 in the 01Z hour and $2.39 in the first 47 minutes of 02Z [measured, `tests/fanout/acctcost.py`]: about $2.5 an hour, $60 over `WARM_FOR`, which alone passes the install's $40 cap. `scripts/cost.py`'s `ReplaceMember and RestorePlacement` line counts Workflow steps, member rows and storage, removed members' days and the spare bucket's packs, and no container hours, so the figure does not count these warms. The cost is per site, not per move: m6aL's restore warmed the restored site a the same way, 25 more containers there, and the two together billed $2.69 in 30 minutes (04:15 to 04:45Z, about $5.4 an hour) with no load, against $0.10 for the 6 minutes after every shard was torn down [measured, `tests/fanout/acctcost.py`]. Left for their `WARM_FOR`, the spare's and site a's would have passed the install's $40 cap within the day. Proposed, not built: the step that ends a site's role (`Home` away from it, or the replace step that removes its member) releases that site's warm (`Caller::Start`'s lapse rather than a client's 24 h); `RestorePlacement` does the same on the spare as it leaves, and once its last group reaches `Done` it releases the restored site's warms too, so only a shard a client then reads stays up. Until it is built, BF-50's rows tear the shards down after `home` (`tests/m6/hosted/bf50-teardown.sh`) | `workers/site/src/member.rs`, `shard_warm`; `crates/git/src/shard.rs`, `WARM_FOR`; `scripts/cost.py`, ReplaceMember | A site loss's cost for a day after it, on every install | A branch with the release and a row that counts the spare's active containers an hour after `home`; and a `scripts/cost.py` term for a move's warm hours |
| R68 | Found building `lifeboat-clone` (2026-11-25): is 3 times the repository bytes the right free-disk floor for a lifeboat's git directory (`cli/src/lifeboat.rs`, `GIT_DISK_TIMES` [guess])? `start.sh` refuses to start when the git directory's free plus used bytes are under 3 times graft-blob's packs plus the copy's state: one for the repositories, one for a repack's new pack beside the old, one for the spool. Unmeasured: a real install's ratio of repository bytes to packs, and whether graft-git's repack ever runs on a lifeboat | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Install operations", Lifeboat | A lifeboat that refuses to start with room enough, or one whose disk fills mid-rebuild, so its clones answer 503 until an operator adds disk | A hosted lifeboat row's repository bytes, git directory size and peak during a rebuild |
| R69 | Found building `lifeboat-clone` (2026-11-25): is the lifeboat's guard on rolls enough? The install's sweeper deletes a roll's consumed packs `CONSUMED_KEEP` after the roll and waits for no lifeboat (the lead's ruling: a lifeboat offline must not block the install's cleanup). A lifeboat holds a consumed pack it already pulled, drops it from `owed` once the produced pack is held, and loads the produced pack in their place. What is not covered: a lifeboat offline past `CONSUMED_KEEP` whose copy holds a roll's consumed packs but not yet its produced pack, then a roll of that produced pack before it returns | `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`lifeboat-clone`" | A lifeboat that comes back to a pack no copy holds, which stays unserved and alarms (`UNSERVED_ALARM_MS`) rather than serving a short clone | A hosted roll observed by a lifeboat row, or a measured roll rate against `CONSUMED_KEEP` |

## Agents

| Id | Question | Stated in | What depends on it | Settled by |

| A1 | Do the base's 300 commands/s gate and the git CPU constants hold under A1's load? The commit half is answered [measured]: four 600 s B0-29 runs at journey 9's 27.6 commands/s gave commit p99 396 to 625 ms, two over the 600 ms pass line and none near the 1 s fail line, merge p99 at most 795 ms, and 0 `Stale` (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`). The git CPU half is answered in part [measured]: B0-34 on torvalds/linux failed with stock clone-cache misses (25 to 46 CPU-s each, human push p99 40.5 s) and passed every human-push phase with composed misses (p99 0.68 to 1.56 s), except clone time to first byte, which reached 19 to 20 s at p99 while the killed site's shards rehydrated. A real test merge costs 5 to 50 ms at p50 and at most 338 ms at p99, so `TEST_MERGE_BUDGET` stays 0.25 (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`, B0-34). The third pass, with both warm sites sized alike and per-move parts, held the kill window at clone time to first byte p99 0.71 to 1.10 s, and `b0-34-r7` gave 0.70 to 1.03 s by phase [measured] | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Open questions and risks" | The A1 verdict: 500 agents on one monorepo group | M0-WP2 (B0-02) and M0-WP9 (B0-29, B0-34) |

Until they pass, such fleets stay off |

B0-33 still fails on leader changes from the `STEP_DOWN` confirm rule and on Human and MergeQueue p99 of 0.85 to 1.1 s (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp9.md`) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Build order from zero" (B0-33 Fail); `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Alternatives considered" (ref lanes) | Whether ref lanes are built | M0-WP9 (B0-33) |

Cloudflare documents overrides for Worker requests and `fetch()` bindings only, and an object keeps its deployment's version [doc], so the design assumes not: a `ControllerPointer` holds boxes on N+1, their Sandboxes stopped, until they run N or the pointer clears, and fails a task held past `HOLD_MAX` | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Open questions and risks" | `ControllerPointer` swap-back for running tasks | Built at $0 (`graftyard_git::taskbox`, plant248). The hold is measured hosted by no row yet; LM-3's kill row (S4) runs the box without a roll |
| A17 | Does a changed Secrets Store value reach a bound script with no new script version [inference from the binding's async `get()`]? | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Open questions and risks" | `graftyard apps init`, App-key rotation, and every `Rotate` | M5-WP6 (BF-46, "No new script version"). **Not answered as asked by BF-46 (2026-11-24, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`, "BF-46 hosted"):** BF-46 never changed a bound value. Each `Rotate` switched `graft-ops` between two store secrets that were both bound at deploy (slots 0 and 1), choosing the slot from its `Release` view, so no Secrets Store value under a bound script changed while it ran. **Half answered (2026-11-24, BF-46 `SshHostKeys` on graft-m3f, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`, m3fs1):** a new value written at about 23:23Z into `SSH_HOST_KEY_B`, which the running site scripts already bound, reached them with no new version: the edge's `/meta/ssh`, which reads it through the site Registry's `/ssh_slots` (cached 60 s per isolate), listed the new public keys within seconds [measured]. **Answered 2026-11-25 [measured, `tests/m5/results/m5-wp6.md`, "A17, the Secrets Store visibility row"]:** yes. Three in-place writes to a bound secret each reached three running isolates (one per location hint, the same isolate id throughout) within 1.1 to 3.5 s of the write's `Date`, and an orb sampler over 65 isolates read no old value 4 s or more after a write. No script version changed. A holder that caches a value in memory sees a write when its cache ends |

One LF-12 run had 0 `overloaded` and another 26 to 36 per phase under the same load. An isolate holds at most 10,000 active timers, so each held poll may keep only a few. Open: how often a client retries a 429, and whether the edge's three tries should stop at the first full member (`tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, LF-12) | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, Shape (feed block, `FEED_HELD_MAX`) | `FEED_HELD_MAX`, a [guess], and replication on every member that serves the feed | M0-WP12 (LF-12) |

The design keeps 0.75 for 3 min and keeps pool shards warm instead, priced at $433 a month at 2,000 developers. B0-34 did not test 0.6 or 1 min: they are inferred from `b0-34-r7` (one shard at 0.6 to 0.82 CPU gave TTFB p99 0.93 to 1.26 s a minute) and `b0-34-r8` (290 s from saturation to k = 2 serving, cold) [inference] | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape (`fanout`, `HOT_POOL`); `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Idle hot-pool shards keep hot networks warm" | The hot pool line in `scripts/cost.py`, and clone time to first byte on a step in load | M0-WP9, a B0-34 rerun at 0.6 and 1 min with a cold pool |
| A23 | Should a follower that stops following spread its held-poll answers over FEED_BATCH, or should `place_learners` run before followers take polls? Such a follower answers up to FEED_HELD_MAX held polls in one second, and their re-polls land in the next. In LF-12 `old-r36`'s no-learner phase, 5 seconds, 53 s apart, each had 1,288 to 2,301 feed requests on both followers, a commit median of 1,098 ms against 261 ms in the other seconds, and follower gaps of 450 to 580 ms against 130 to 190 ms; `r35` and `r29` show the same (664 against 318 ms, and 744 against 327 ms). No gate missed on it [measured, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp12.md`, tenth pass, "Tenth-pass proposed design edits" 33] | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, Shape (feed block) | LF-12's no-learner phase, and any group whose followers hold polls before learners are placed | No work package yet. | A37 | Found in B0-32 m3fgb1 (2026-11-21, F106 reopened): may a leader's read-index grant (`ReadIndexRes`) leave while an unrelated log write is still unconfirmed, without breaking the acknowledgment rule? **What a grant states.** It names a commit index, `fold.applied()` at the grant (`check_reads`), which is at or below a commit that held the quorum rule; `advance_commit` commits nothing above `own_durable`, so the leader's own copy of every entry through it is confirmed. And it names leadership, confirmed by the lease or a read round answered after the read arrived. Neither rests on the transaction pending at the grant: that write holds entries above the commit index, `MemberState` (term, `commit`, `applied`) and fold rows for applies the confirm will cover. If it is lost, the object resets, and its restarted member resumes `led_term` with a commit index no lower than any grant it gave, because the commit index granted was quorum-durable before the write. So the grant depends on the pending entry's durability only through `applied`, which is quorum-committed already [inference, read from `crates/raft/src/member/leader.rs`, `check_reads`, `advance_commit`, and `on_read_index`]. **How a DO could send it.** The output gate holds every outgoing message behind every earlier write that asked for confirmation (`ActorSqlite::onWrite`: a write without `allowUnconfirmed` locks the gate until its commit confirms). SQL statements always ask: `transactionSync` calls `notifyWrite()` with the default `false`, and `sql.exec` takes no option; only the KV `put`, `delete` and alarm calls accept `allowUnconfirmed` [doc, workerd `src/workerd/io/actor-sqlite.c++` and `src/workerd/api/actor-state.c++` at `84c16be`; https://developers.cloudflare.com/durable-objects/api/sqlite-storage-api/]. So nothing narrower than the whole object's output is available: a grant cannot be sent past the gate while the log write is SQL. Doing it needs the log write itself unconfirmed, which the platform offers only through the KV API on the same SQLite (`__cf_kv`), and then every path that today rests on the gate would have to await `sync()` itself. **Paths that would then send before their own write confirms:** AppendEntries (safe: the leader counts itself only through `SelfAck` after `sync()`, already); a follower's `AppendRes` and vote replies (unsafe unless each awaits `sync()`: a follower's ack counts toward quorum and a vote must be stored before it leaves); `ReadIndexRes` and the leader's own read answers (safe by the argument above); `/commit` `admitted` and `/outcome_by_request` `pending` (safe, A34: they promise nothing stored); every 2xx from `outcome` (safe only if it awaits `sync()` past its entry: `Acked` is built after the quorum rule, which already counts the leader's `SelfAck`, so the reply rests on no later write [inference]); the `request` row behind a duplicate's replay, alarms, snapshots and the shard's cursor writes (each needs its own audit). The store, statement and atomicity changes of a KV log are a new storage format, not an option on today's. **What it would buy on m3fgb1** [inference, `tests/fanout/grantgate.py`]: of each member's round trip to the leader, at least p50 260 / p90 801 ms passed before the leader could have handled it, and at most p50 595 / p90 1,094 ms after. Replayed for gate reads starting T+2.7 to 6.0 s, `gate_rows`' repository read goes from p50 2,927 ms (43% at 3 s or more) to p50 300 to 541 ms (0 to 1%), and the pushes' `gate_rows` from p50 4,210 to about 288. The leader also took no input from T+2.97 to 5.08 s inside the confirm, which no output rule touches, so the true gain is nearer the least-gain replay. | `design/DESIGN.md`, Shape, "The write path" and "Acknowledgment point"; Commit and read paths, "Follower read"; `workers/site/src/host.rs`, `flush` and `settled`; `crates/raft/src/member/leader.rs`, `check_reads` | B0-32's within-5 s line (308 `full` at the bound on m3fgb1) and every hot group's read gate during a slow leader confirm | No work package. An operator decision on a KV-backed log (a new storage format) or on taking the loss; before either, a row that splits the leader's input stop from its output hold inside a slow confirm (a `CallLeft`-to-`AnswerArrived` gap with the leader's clock checked against its callers'), since if the input stop is most of it the change buys little. **Instrument row B0-32 m3fhs1 (2026-11-21):** no leader confirm reached 1 s (largest 348 ms), so it split none. Re-read with the leader's Workers Logs events (`tests/fanout/leaderwl.py`), m3fgb1 corrects this note: the leader's clock was not frozen (the callers' clocks ran ahead), it took input throughout (longest gap 818 ms), and its `/raft` responses were held p50 560 to 731 ms after the handler returned inside the 2,457 ms confirm, against p50 5 to 195 ms outside it [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Row m3fhs1"]. So a grant past the gate would save about 0.6 to 0.7 s of each member's round trip in a slow confirm, not all of it, and the replay above overstates the gain [inference]. **Closed by the operator (2026-11-21): not worth a KV log.** About 0.6 to 0.7 s per member round trip, only during a confirm over 1 s, does not pay for a new storage format and a `sync()` audit on every gated path, and m3fhs1 failed B0-32 (364 of 500) with no slow confirm at all (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "Not adopted: a KV-backed log so read grants leave past the output gate") |

| A41 | Found in B0-33's control rows (2026-11-22, `tests/am1/results/nochange-row.md`, "Slower host or more work?"): does a group leader's speed depend on the host its Durable Object lands on, enough to decide a hosted gate? Across five m64-shaped rows on graft-m2b, the leader object (`#1#f0`, site a) ran in a different colo each time: EWR (sabA), IAD (m64r33, chA), ORD (crA) and ATL (ncA). At the same inputs (306 to 347 a second, 225 on m64r33's shorter window) and the same CPU (32 to 41 CPU-seconds a loaded minute, 0.55 to 0.69 vCPU), with no more turn work per input than sabA's, its ticker stalls of 500 ms or more ran 2.0 (EWR), 10.0 and 16.2 (IAD), 7.2 (ORD) and 19.2 (ATL) a minute, and its confirm p90 379 to 826 ms [measured, `tests/fanout/hostwork.py` and Durable Object analytics by `coloCode`]. The stalls are time the isolate had work and did not run it, not extra CPU, so the cause is the host it shares (scheduling or contention) [inference]. Edge traffic to the leader did not change with `colo-gate`, and the edge's `/suspect` reports followed the stalls, so neither is the cause. Host speed is inferred from stalls at equal CPU, not measured as a machine, and IAD alone gave 10.0 and 16.2 a minute, so placement is not the whole spread [inference] **Correction, 2026-11-22 (B0-33 second opinion; `tests/am1/results/b033-percmd.md`, "The leader's clock"): the stall counts and confirm p90s above are leader-clock readings and are not durations.** Under this load the leader's `Date.now()` runs behind the near voter's clock: the leader stamps a voter's answer arriving 0.3 to 3.1 s before the voter stamped it leaving (`back` = leader `AnswerArrived` minus voter `CallLeft`, per 30 s bucket: idle +32 to +111 ms; loaded sabA −541 to −1,426, blB −386 to −1,056, blA −1,088 to −2,196, blC2 −679 to −3,126), and net of idle it reads 1.2 to 2.4 s behind at p50 (p90 1.8 to 3.8 s) on sabA, blA, blB and blC2, against about 0 on sync-ab's arm B2 at 168 inputs/s [measured, `tests/fanout/b033probes/host/drift.py` and `stale.py`]. A busy isolate's clock reads its last I/O (`tests/am1/results/lease-clock.md`), so a ticker "stall" and a confirm's wall are the loop running late, not a storage or host duration [inference]. sync-ab's confirm result (p90 379 → 136 → 101 ms, `decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`sync-ab`") rests on the same clock: it shows the leader's loop got less late as its inputs fell, not that confirms got 3.75× shorter [inference]. On the near voter's clock alone (`second.py`: next entry's arrival less the voter's last answer leaving, less the idle wire), leader excess p50 / p90 per cycle is 360 / 763 ms (sabA, EWR) and 373 / 661 (blB, EWR), against 427 / 857 (crA, ORD), 446 / 1,057 to 480 / 1,244 (blC2, blA, chA, IAD) and 703 / 1,433 (ncA, ATL) [measured]. | A55 | Found in LF-8's hosted row r1 (2026-11-25, `tests/am3/results/lf8.md`, "Replays"). **Answered by the lead's ruling (2026-11-25, DECISIONS "`agents-equivocation`"): yes, and built [measured natively, `crates/release/src/tests/group/agents.rs`, `a55_other_bytes_at_a_held_index_are_equivocation_and_the_same_bytes_are_skipped`; `tests/m0carried/plant228.py`].** The view keeps each applied index's digest; other bytes at a held index are refused as `Equivocation { index }`, which pages in `graft-agents-front` and the agents `graft-ops`; equal bytes are skipped as before; the view and pin do not move. The hosted re-read waits for a later LF-8 row. Should the agents view alarm on a conflicting entry at an index it has already applied? `AgentsReleaseView::extend` skips every entry at or below its index without comparing bytes, so 25 replays of index 10 and 25 of index 9, each carrying bytes other than the held entry's, each counted as an ok refresh (`oks` 2 to 51, `fails` unchanged) and logged nothing [measured]. That is right for a member behind the view, which answers the same bytes, but a different entry at an applied index means a member, or whatever answers as the host, serves a second history, and the front cannot tell that apart from staleness. ## Costs

| Id | Question | Stated in | What depends on it | Settled by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| K1 | What does a Spectrum Worker-origin application cost? No price is published for Worker apps [doc: none found, `tests/m0/results/m0-wp7.md`, B0-24c]. The SSH option prices its data as Spectrum's SSH app, 5 GB a month on Pro (10 GB on Business) then $1 per GB [unverified for Worker apps], plus a $0 Worker-app fee line. The Spectrum add-on on Enterprise no longer gates native SSH | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Cost" (the Spectrum line and the SSH-option paragraph), "Tradeoffs accepted", "Open questions and risks", and "Review resolutions" (CF-ACT-13); `alternatives/AWS.md`, "Cost" | `scripts/cost.py` (`SPECTRUM_ADDON_USD`, `SSH_OPTION_PLANS`, `--ssh-option`) and `research/costs.md`. SSH is off by default, so the tier totals do not depend on it; with it on, Spectrum data is $624 of the $664 added at 2,000 developers | No work package. The first Spectrum invoice line on `graftyard.dev` with SSH on, or a published price |
| K2 | Is container traffic back to its own Durable Object billed as egress, as `cost.py` assumes? Partly measured: with `enableInternet: false`, `containersUsageAdaptiveGroups.txBytes` counted 541 GB for the account from 2026-11-01 to 18 [measured, read-only GraphQL], so the meter counts those bytes; no region passed its allowance, so the invoice is unconfirmed | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Open questions and risks" | The egress line in `scripts/cost.py`. If it is not billed, the egress line ($1,708 at 2,000 developers) falls by about half; `edge-compose` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`) shrinks it either way | An invoice from a month whose egress passes an allowance |
| K3 | What do model tokens cost per Graftyard-run task, where $2 a task is a [guess] that dominates a platform engineer's bill? | `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, "Open questions and risks" | The model-token line, about $182,400 a month at 2,000 developers (`scripts/cost_agents.py`), and the controller's `TaskBudget` | M0-WP14 (side measurement per harness) |
| K4 | What does an Enterprise contract cost, given that it has no list price and that managed cells and pushes over 200 MB need it? | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Cost"; `alternatives/AWS.md`, "Cost" | Managed mode cost and BYOC installs with pushes over 200 MB | No work package |
| K5 | Will a 50-developer install accept that a Region loss is not RPO zero, for AWS `scrappy` at $226 a month against the Cloudflare base's $238? | `alternatives/AWS.md`, condition 7 | The choice of platform for small installs | No work package. The operator decides |
| K6 | Do idle groups bill Durable Object duration? Answered no by B0-15 [measured]: over 24 hours across a UTC midnight, 100,000 idle groups (300,000 members in three regions) showed 0 requests, 0 GB-s, and 0 active time in Cloudflare's analytics, and 0 `Rtt` rechecks in every member's own report, while 1,000 control groups served 2.95 M requests and 88,939 GB-s and rechecked 24 to 25 times per member (`tests/m0/results/b0-15-20261027T1552.json`) | `design/DESIGN.md`, "Build order from zero" (B0-15) | The cost of holding many idle groups in `scripts/cost.py` [inference] | M0-WP1 (B0-15), done 2026-10-28 |

The script is unchanged, so the figures are upper bounds [inference] |
| K8 | What keeps torn-down git cache shards down when no load runs? On `graft-m2b` they ran again within the hour at about $3/h for the set, woken by `PrSync` redeliveries that retry a failing merge without end (189 `shard/merge` calls in 30 idle minutes), `warm()` posting `shard/catchup` on each new leadership, and the `*/15` fold audit restoring old members, which starts the second [inference] [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, section 4; `tests/am1/results/lm1-rerun2.md`] | `build/BUILD-PLAN.md`, AM1-WP1 ("Cost risk: idle shard wake-ups") | The container line in `scripts/cost.py`, which bills shards only while they serve, and every hosted round's cost | Branch `idle-shards` (thread T-01a1c8aa-e61f-7739-bd38-27cb59655101) |
| K9 | What do learners cost at journey 9, now that it places them? `scripts/cost.py` prices learners at zero because "no group reaches 500 reads a second" [guess], and journey A1 counts the group's reads at 322/s under `LEARNER_AT`. But `GroupLoad` now counts journey 9's whole-tier reads of about 1 MB 6 times each [inference], because a follower shed them past about 170 reads/s [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, sections 10 and 11], so about 200 API reads/s weigh about 1,200 and pass `LEARNER_AT`, and LM-1's round 4 ran 8 learners per row while members still shed about half the API reads; in round 5, 8 learners and 2 followers shed 29 to 71% of `read:api` per row at `HEAVY_READS` [measured, `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, section 12], so the learners journey 9 needs, and their cost, are not yet known. Each learner is a Durable Object holding both tiers (about 8 GiB of SQLite at A1's 30 days) and serving its reads, none of it priced | `design/DESIGN.md`, Cost ("Learners cost nothing at these tiers") and Scale ("Hot group reads, learners", agent fleet row); `design/AGENT-LAYER.md`, journey A1 (step 4) and Scale (activity feed row); `scripts/cost.py` | The agent cost lines and the 2,000-developer total, and how many learners `place_learners` adds to a hot repository group | Closed 2026-11-12 on branch `learner-dumps` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "K9: learners serve whole-tier reads from one shared dump, and are priced"). The cause was the caps, not learner capacity: at `HEAVY_READS` 8 learners shed 74% of `read:api` at 9 to 20% CPU. Learners now share one dump per tier state and are not held to `HEAVY_READS`, and 8 served 94.7% of 500 agents' API reads on `graft-m3c`, about 19 a second each at 18 to 35% CPU [measured, `tests/am1/results/learner-dumps.md`]. `GroupLoad` counted a whole-tier read 50 times, so A1 placed 11 learners at $237 a month; since narrow-reads an API read is narrow and counts once, so A1 places none on cost grounds and the line is $0 (`scripts/cost_agents.py`; `tests/am1/results/narrow-reads.md`) |
| K10 | What does the daily fold audit's refold cost in rows written, and can its scratch writes be bounded or batched? On `graft-m2b` site a's `*/15` audit (trace 06fae83a, 2026-11-17) refolded `repo:65536` (`m2b/bf20`, a 31.6 MB core snapshot of 571,889 rows, index 7,335, 16 batches, 31,610,182 bytes read), and the leader, `repo:65536#1#f1` on site b, wrote 1,144,665 rows in the minute 09:06Z, about 2.0 rows per core-tier row and about 36,000 rows per MB: about $1.15 at $1 per million, once a day per chain (`AUDIT_EVERY_MS`, 24 h) [measured, `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups` by object, Workers Logs `fold audit refold`; `tests/am1/results/lm1-memory.md`, "The 32 live finished groups retired and purged"]. `AUDIT_SNAPSHOT_MAX` (256 MB, F52) skips only larger tiers, so a chain under it pays this every day. `scripts/cost.py` has no fold rows per repository chain and no audit line, so the audit is unpriced. Its rate is bounded by `SAMPLE` (4 chains a run, `fold_audit.rs`): 3 sites x 96 runs x 4 = at most 1,152 refolds a day, each about 2 rows written per fold row of the chain's core tier. At 65536's size that bound is about 1.3 G rows, $1,319, a day; a chain is refolded at most once a day, so the real figure is the sum over the audited chains of 2 x their core rows, and the large tier's 19,072 groups (`cost.py`) would each be refolded about every 17 days at that rate [inference from the measured rows per refold and the code's caps]. On `graft-m3f` (2026-11-18), 40 finished test repositories (`repo:65537` to `65576`, left joined by runs from 2026-11-13 to 11-16) were refolded one site-a run of 4 at a time from 01:48Z, and from 03:30Z to 04:20Z the 16 of them refolded then wrote 501,211 rows, about $0.50: per chain 40 to 130,536 rows (median about 1,100), 1 to 14 rows for a chain whose refold found no new index, and 90,588 to 130,536 for `repo:65568` to `65571` (core indices 1,300 to 4,726, refold bytes up to 106.5 MB). Of each large chain's rows, about 9,000 to 10,000 were written by each of its 10 non-leader members as well as its leader (`repo:65571#1#f0` 118,523), so a chain with learners pays about 11 times the leader's share [measured, `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups` by object; Workers Logs `fold audit` on `graft-m3f-site-a`; `tests/am1/results/install-m3f.md`, 2026-11-18 idle-rows row]. Those 9,000 to 10,000 rows are not refold work: the refold runs on the leader alone (`audit_route` refuses `start` elsewhere). They are each member's one-time expiry of the run's `request` rows (`DELETE FROM request WHERE at < cut`, REQUEST_RETENTION 48 h, `workers/site/src/host.rs`), run when the first entry more than 48 h after the run applies. On an idle chain that entry is the no-op of a term the audit's own lookup started (`graftyard_lookup::call` campaigns a leaderless group), and the followers apply it only at the next visit, when the next append carries the commit: `repo:65568` (B0-31 m3fr6, 2026-11-15 21:17Z) wrote 33 rows on the leader at the 11-17 21:18Z visit, 9,054 on the leader at 00:48Z, and 9,053 to 9,064 on each of its 10 other members at 03:48Z [inference from the per-object rows and the visit times; the members are retired, so the row count was not read]. So a chain pays its request expiry once, not per refold, and every product chain pays it too. What the audit adds daily on an idle chain is the election: a new term's no-op on every member, 3 to 43 rows each per visit, and the commit index climbs with no write (`repo:65548` 2,922 to 2,942). Measured per refold on `graft-m3f` (2026-11-18, leader rows only): `repo:65563` 14,846 (2.6 MB read), `65569` 18,622, `65570` 34,229 (9.5 MB, 1,287 entries), `65566` 34,943 (8.2 MB, 2,442 entries), `65571` 118,523 (106.5 MB, 4,696 entries), 14 to 27 rows per log entry refolded, $0.015 to $0.12 per chain per refold at $1 per million. Can the refold be bounded (stream a digest without scratch tables, or a cap in rows rather than snapshot bytes) or batched across chains? Since `audit-moved` (2026-11-18), a chain is refolded only if it wrote its fold since its last matching audit, an idle chain is compared from the voters' digest rings with no write and no election, and a tombstoned one is skipped; `scripts/cost.py` prices the rest as "Fold audit refolds" ($30, $390, and $482 a month at the three tiers, `AUDIT_MOVED_SHARE` 0.3 [guess]) (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "audit-moved"). Left open: the moved share, and whether a moved chain's refold can be bounded or batched. **Row size, measured 2026-11-18.** The line first used `ROW_KB` 4, a [guess] made for `ReplaceMember`'s snapshot copies, which gave $30, $390, and $482 a month. On `graft-m4a`'s `repo:65536` (j4, read-only through the tools edge by M4-WP3 at 14:01Z), (dbBytes - logBytes) / fold rows is 619 to 622 B on voters `#1#f0` and `#1#f2` (3,838,681 B over 6,171 metadata and 32 core rows) and 740 B on the lost voter `#2#f1`; every voter's paged refold matched its kept digest. That is an upper bound on bytes per row (dbBytes also holds request rows, FTS5, and snap_log; logBytes is the entries' encoded size, not the log table's), so a lower bound on rows per GB. The same tier's snapshot frames hold 65 B per metadata row and 129 B per core row, a floor on bytes; m2-wp1's seeded comments took 2,997 B of SQLite a row [measured]. One dbBytes cannot be split by tier and j4's core tier is 32 rows, so `ROW_KB` is 0.62 for both tiers, and the core tier's row size is unmeasured. `scripts/cost.py` now gives $421, $2,517, and $3,110 a month for the line (the largest at 50 and 300 developers), and base totals of $656, $3,726, and $12,463; at the 65 B floor the line would be $4,372, $24,010, and $29,660. This makes the refold's scratch writes, not its cadence, the cost to fix: a refold that streams a digest without writing rows, or a cap in rows, would remove most of the line. **Scratch writes removed, 2026-11-18 (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`refold-stream`").** The refold now stages each snapshot as 256 KB pages and applies the tail over them, writing each page and each changed row once and deleting both at its end. `scripts/cost.py` prices it by rows written and read, R2 range reads and duration: $0.01, $1.48, and $9.03 a month at 620 B a row ($0.10, $8.02, and $17.10 at the 65 B floor), and base totals of $235, $1,211, and $9,362. Since K12's re-price and the measured fixed rows per audit (site day m3frs, 2026-11-18), the line is $0.01, $2.26, and $9.29 ($0.10, $8.80, and $17.37 at the floor) and the totals $235, $1,280, and $9,819. One hosted refold's `rowsWritten` was read on that day: 60 and 52 rows for 6 pages with 11 and 14 saved changes (`tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`, "Site day m3frs"). Left open: the moved share. | `workers/site/src/load.rs` (`AUDIT_EVERY_MS`, `AUDIT_SNAPSHOT_MAX`, `reset_scratch`), `workers/site/src/fold_audit.rs`; `design/SHARED.md`, "Folds" | The audit's place in the monthly bill; whether it refolds into scratch tables, streams a digest without writing, or audits a sample of rows | No work package yet. A cost line in `scripts/cost.py` with a measured rows-per-chain input, and a design choice for the refold's writes |
| K11 | Should every member's live tier rows be checked against the digest its `apply` kept, weekly? The fold audit compares digests that `apply` computed, and its refold reads no member's rows, so a row changed in storage after `apply` (a bad write, or storage corruption) is caught by neither, before or after `audit-moved`. `refold_digest` (`crates/fold/src/tier.rs`) rehashes a tier's rows, and `refold_page` (`/fold {"refold": ..}`) runs it on demand in pages [measured in code, not run on a schedule]. A weekly pass per member would read every row of both tiers once a week, with no rows written | `crates/fold/src/tier.rs` (`refold_digest`), `workers/site/src/snap.rs` (`refold_page`), `workers/site/src/fold_audit.rs` | Whether `audit-moved`'s flip on storage corruption can ever fire, and the audit's read cost | Answered by `live-check` (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, 2026-11-18): yes, each voter's both tiers once per `LIVE_EVERY_MS` (7 days [guess]), as a snapshot job with no upload, from the fold audit's cron; priced at $0.76, $4.31 and $35.52 a month. Learners are not checked (F94). Built code-only; the hosted half rides the next `graft-m3f` site day. Closed by `live-check` |
| K12 | Does a member's own tier table cost two rows written per row put? The fold tier tables (`workers/site/src/tier.rs`, `create`) have a composite `PRIMARY KEY` and no `WITHOUT ROWID`, so SQLite keeps the row in the rowid table and its key in an automatic index, and each put may write both [inference, from the schema and SQLite's file format; Lumenize's Durable Object write-cost measurements, https://lumenize.com/blog/do-sqlite-write-costs [doc, secondary]]. The refold that restored into scratch tables of the same shape wrote 2.0 rows per tier row [measured, K10]. `scripts/cost.py` prices `apply`'s writes at `FOLD_ROWS` 6 rows per entry with no such factor. Not changed by `refold-stream`, whose own tables are `WITHOUT ROWID` | `scripts/cost.py`, `FOLD_ROWS`; `workers/site/src/tier.rs` | The "Durable Objects SQLite rows written" line ($270 at 2,000 developers), which would double; whether the tier tables move to `WITHOUT ROWID` as a new table set and refold (`design/SHARED.md`, "Schema change is a refold, never a migration") | Measurement: `rowsWritten` for one `apply` of a known entry on a seeded repository at the next `graft-m3f` site day (`tests/am1/results/lm1-rows-m3f.md`). **Measured 2026-11-18 (site day m3frs, `repo:65588`):** one `DefineLabel` entry, 6 fold rows put in memory, wrote 19 rows on the leader in each of 4 quiet minutes [measured]. workerd counts one row per `OP_Insert` and one per `OP_IdxInsert` (`patches/sqlite/0001-row-counts-plain.patch` in cloudflare/workerd [doc]), so each fold put is 2. With `log` 1, `request` 2, `raft_state` 1 or 2, and 2 test-only `arrival` rows, a product member writes about 17 rows for this entry, against `cost.py`'s 7 [inference]. At 17 rows an entry, the rows-written line would be $0, $66 and $726 a month at 50, 300 and 2,000 developers, and the totals $235, $1,282 and $9,832. Priced on 2026-11-18 at `ENTRY_ROWS` 17 (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`k12-rows`"): the line is $0, $66 and $726 and the totals $235, $1,280 and $9,819; `WITHOUT ROWID` deferred with a flip. Closed by site day m3frs. **Re-priced 2026-11-19 by family and kind** (`tests/entryrows/run.sh`, local workerd's `rows_written()` over the member's own statements) [measured]: the bed's `DefineLabel` writes 14, and with `raft_state`'s second write 15 and 16 on members, as hosted; a repository entry averages 21.7 rows per member and an Actions, Identity, or Tokens entry 8.3, and the latter are 85% of entries. Rows written are $0, $20 and $420 a month in the base and $0, $84 and $563 in the agent layer; totals $238, $1,016 and $9,325, with agents $259, $1,344 and $12,256 (`decisions/DECISIONS.md`, "`k12-rows`" and "`without-rowid`") |
| K13 | Found on graft-m4a's idle drift (2026-11-22): which statements does a repository member write when the fold audit wakes it, and should a restore write anything beyond what it changes? From 17:48Z to 20:32Z, with no load and no container, site a's `*/15` fold audit visited 4 of its 16 repository chains a run, so each chain about hourly. Each visit woke the chain's 3 voters, 12 members a wake, and each wrote about 20,400 (17:48Z), 23,900 (19:03Z) and 29,500 (20:02Z) rows in the minute of its first `/raft` call, on members near index 13,000 (`repo:65578` 12,983, `65579` 13,630). Between wakes the 288 members wrote about 2,000 to 6,000 rows per 5 minutes. 20:02Z to 20:32Z cost $0.39, $0.38 of it rows written [measured, `durableObjectsPeriodicGroups` by object and minute; Workers Logs `fold audit` on `graft-m4a-site-a`; `tests/am1/results/install-m4a.md`, 2026-11-22 20:49Z row]. **Traced from the same data, at $0:** each waking object wrote 8.4% to 8.9% of the rows it wrote in the hour exactly 48 h earlier (11 objects at 20:02Z against 2026-11-20 19:03Z to 20:03Z, 11 at 19:03Z against 18:03Z to 19:03Z; one leader 5.4% and 11.8%), and in the hours after that load ended the same objects wrote about 400 rows an hour [measured]. The wakes write no restore rows. Each visit's lookup starts a term, and its no-op, at the wake's time, is the first entry each follower applies. It runs `DELETE FROM request WHERE at < cut` (REQUEST_RETENTION 48 h, `host::SqlStore`), so each hourly visit expires the `request` rows of the next hour of B0-35's load 48 h before. That is the per-entry expiry `scripts/cost.py` already prices in `REPO_DELETE_ROWS`, paid in batches when an idle chain next applies an entry instead of entry by entry [inference from the matching ratio, and K10's per-chain expiry on `graft-m3f`]. Growth across visits is the load's shape 48 h earlier. At 2,000 developers (8,000 repository chains, `scripts/cost.py`), the audit visits at most 3 sites x 4 chains every 15 minutes: 48 chains and 144 voters an hour, each chain about weekly. Those wakes add no rows beyond the priced expiry and a no-op of 3 to 43 rows per member (K10), about 6,000 rows, $0.006, an hour [inference]. **Confirmed in local workerd [measured, 2026-11-22, scratch trace on `m5-wp4-host`]:** the site's own `test-faults` build as three scripts (exportbed's wiring), one register chain loaded to index 12,966 by 13,900 commits one at a time, `GRAFTYARD_REQUEST_RETENTION_S` 300, and each statement's `rows_written()` counted in `commit_tx`. Every member reset (`/abort`), then one campaign, as the audit's lookup starts. Restore itself (`Host::restore`'s `persist`) wrote nothing on all three members. The wake's inputs wrote, per member: the vote and the no-op's append, `log` 2 and `raft_state` 1; then the no-op's apply, `DELETE FROM request WHERE at < cut` and `raft_state` 1. With no `request` row past retention the apply wrote 1 row (wake at 21:55:29Z); 300 s later the same wake's apply wrote 2,284, all but 1 of them the expiry: 2,283 `request` rows deleted (13,900 to 11,617 on each member), one row written per row deleted. So a restore writes nothing beyond what it changes, and the wake's rows are the priced expiry, paid in a batch. No code fix | `workers/site/src/host.rs` (`Host::restore`, `SqlStore`, the `request` expiry), `workers/site/src/fold_audit.rs` | Whether the "Durable Objects SQLite rows written" line misses a restore cost. It does not | **Settled** by the hosted trace and the local count. No cost-model change: the audit's cadence is unchanged, and an idle chain's wakes add about 6 rows a member beyond the expiry `REPO_DELETE_ROWS` already prices |
